## U-20260922-09 · 2026-09-22 · Eight plugins had no menu entry; name the Extra Tools submenus · #incident #plugins #Imervue_Plugins

- **What**: npr_filters, ai_colorize, ai_denoise, ai_motion_deblur, ai_portrait_relight, ai_smart_resize, ai_style_transfer and portrait_mode (all added from 2026-04-29 on) searched the argument of `on_build_menu_bar` for the top-level "Extra Tools" menu. That argument has been the shared Plugins `QMenu` since 2026-04-06 (`3e9d0c6`), so they never found it, added no entry anywhere, and had no other hook: loaded but unreachable. The host now gives the Extra Tools menu and its eight submenus stable object names (`extra_tools`, `extra_tools.<key>`, `Imervue/menu/extra_tools_menu.py` `submenu_object_name`). The plugins look their submenu up with `main_window.findChild(QMenu, ...)` and fall back to the Plugins menu on a host that predates the names, so Imervue 1.0.90 users get an entry as well. The names are recorded as a contract in `architecture.md` §6. While there, npr_filters (OpenCV) and portrait_mode (rembg, onnxruntime) now open their dialog through `ensure_dependencies`, so a missing package is offered for install instead of failing at Apply. The eight plugins go to 1.0.1 and are mirrored to Imervue_Plugins `main`.
- **Result / numbers**: offscreen probe of the real main window (language Traditional_Chinese): before, none of the eight titles appeared in any menu while the controls (AI 去背, Icon 工具) did; after, each sits in 額外功能 › 修復與變形 or 額外功能 › 調整（非破壞性）. New tests: `tests/test_plugin_menu_placement.py` (16, real `QMenuBar`, entry placement and trigger wiring, old-host fallback), `tests/test_plugin_dependency_gate.py` (8), three in `tests/test_extra_tools_menu.py`.
- **Found on the way**: Qt keeps only a weak reference to a bound-method slot, so a plugin instance that nobody holds silently stops receiving `triggered` (the plugin manager holds them; noted in `PLUGIN_DEV_GUIDE.md`). Plugins → Reload Plugins now duplicates these entries.
- **Files**: `Imervue/menu/extra_tools_menu.py`, `plugins/{npr_filters,ai_colorize,ai_denoise,ai_motion_deblur,ai_portrait_relight,ai_smart_resize,ai_style_transfer,portrait_mode}/`, the three test files, `PLUGIN_DEV_GUIDE.md`, `architecture.md`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.
- **Open items**: `progress.md` #13 (optional ONNX paths offer no install), #14 (reload duplicates entries).

## U-20260922-10 · 2026-09-22 · Plugin downloader: two categories, one API request · #done #plugins #Imervue_Plugins

- **What**: `Imervue/plugin/plugin_downloader.py` treated every top-level non-dot directory of Imervue_Plugins as a plugin category, so that repository could hold nothing else (its update log lives here for that reason). Only `plugins` and `languages` count now (`PLUGIN_CATEGORIES`). The listing also changed from one Contents API call per directory to a single recursive git-tree call (`REPO_TREE_URL`), grouped by the pure function `parse_plugin_tree`. It keeps only files directly inside `<category>/<plugin>/`, skips hidden and dunder directories, sorts by category then name, and raises on a truncated listing. Files still come from raw.githubusercontent, now with URL-quoted paths. Closes `progress.md` #11 and workspace X-18.
- **Result / numbers**: opening the dialog used 1 + 2 + 17 = 20 GitHub API requests out of the 60 per hour an unauthenticated client gets, so the third open within an hour could hit the limit. It now uses 1. A live run against the repository listed the same 17 plugins as the local mirror, and a raw download succeeded. `tests/test_plugin_downloader.py`: 16 tests; `tests/test_plugin.py` updated for the new listing.
- **Still true**: downloaders released before this change keep the old rule, so Imervue_Plugins must not gain other top-level directories until those versions are out of use (`architecture.md` §6).
- **Evidence**: GitHub REST "Get a tree" (`recursive`, `truncated`, 100,000 entries / 7 MB), unauthenticated primary rate limit 60 requests per hour.
- **Files**: `Imervue/plugin/plugin_downloader.py`, `tests/test_plugin_downloader.py`, `tests/test_plugin.py`, `architecture.md`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260922-11 · 2026-09-22 · Concurrent pytest runs deleted each other's tmp_path · #incident #tests

- **What**: a full run turned every test from 70 % on into a setup error (`E`) after a second, short pytest run started beside it. `tests/conftest.py` `_prune_old_pytest_basetemps` runs at collection and removes all but the newest three `pytest-of-<user>/pytest-<N>` directories. It ignored the `.lock` file pytest writes into a basetemp while its session runs, so the second run deleted the first run's `tmp_path` root. The prune now skips a directory whose `.lock` is younger than pytest's own three-day lock timeout, and treats an unreadable lock as live. `base` became a parameter so the helper is testable.
- **Result / numbers**: with the fix, a full run with several short runs beside it finished with no setup errors (10,991 passed; the one failure was an unrelated outdated test, U-20260922-10). `tests/test_conftest_basetemp_prune.py`: 5 tests.
- **Files**: `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_conftest_basetemp_prune.py`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260922-13 · 2026-09-22 · Offer onnxruntime before the ONNX paths of six plugins · #done #plugins #Imervue_Plugins

- **What**: ai_colorize, ai_denoise, ai_motion_deblur, ai_portrait_relight, ai_style_transfer and ai_object_remove run an ONNX model only when the user picks one, and without onnxruntime they ended in an ImportError toast with no way to install it. Each dialog's commit now routes the ONNX branch through `ensure_dependencies(self, ONNX_PACKAGES, self._start_worker)` and starts the non-ONNX branch at once. `_start_worker` returns early when the dialog is no longer visible, because the callback can arrive after the user closed it. ai_style_transfer is ONNX only and always checks. ai_object_remove checks before a SAM click (`_run_sam` → `_start_sam`) and before an ONNX inpaint. Versions: 1.0.2 (five plugins), ai_object_remove 1.0.1. Closes `progress.md` #13.
- **Result / numbers**: `tests/test_plugin_onnx_gate.py`, 25 tests, drives the routing on a stand-in `self`, so no dialog, model discovery or image load is needed. Full suite: 11,028 passed, 1 skipped.
- **Files**: `plugins/{ai_colorize,ai_denoise,ai_motion_deblur,ai_portrait_relight,ai_style_transfer,ai_object_remove}/`, `tests/test_plugin_onnx_gate.py`, `progress.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260922-15 · 2026-09-22 · QAction.menu() invalidated cached menus; plugin reload fixed · #done #pyside6 #plugins

- **What**: in PySide6 6.11.0 and 6.11.1, `QAction.menu()` re-parents the returned `QMenu` wrapper under the temporary `QAction` wrapper in shiboken's ownership tree (`shiboken6.dump` shows the parent change). Once that action wrapper is collected, shiboken invalidates the menu wrapper and every reference cached elsewhere, although the C++ menu is alive. `ImervueMainWindow._enable_tooltips_on_all_menus` walked the bar this way during construction, so `main_window.language_menu` was already dead when plugin init tried to append plugin languages, and Español never reached the Language menu (`progress.md` #3). The command palette and the paint shortcut sheet walked menus the same way, invalidating `_plugin_menu` and the paint tab's `_<key>_menu`.
- **Fix**: `Imervue/gui/menu_tree.py` resolves submenus through `findChildren(QMenu)` and `menuAction()` (`submenu_index`, `submenu_of`, `iter_menu_actions`). The tooltip walk, the command palette and `paint/shortcuts_dialog.py` use it, and `_safe_submenus_of` is gone. The Language and Plugins menus have object names (`language_menu`, `plugin_menu`) and are found with `findChild`. safety_review looks up the shared "AI Tools" submenu with `findChildren` (1.0.1).
- **Reload** (`progress.md` #14): `dispatch_plugin_menus` records every entry the plugin hooks add, to the Plugins menu, an Extra Tools submenu or the bar. Plugins → Reload Plugins removes and deletes those entries before loading again, so they no longer pile up or call unloaded instances. A removed submenu is detached before `deleteLater`; otherwise a reloaded plugin that looks up a shared submenu with `findChildren` filled the doomed copy, and the Plugins menu dropped from 18 to 13 entries in the probe.
- **Result / numbers**: offscreen probes of the real main window: the Language menu lists 英文 … 日文, separator, Español; one reload leaves every menu's entry count unchanged. The repro gives the cached wrapper as invalid on 6.11.0 (Python 3.11) and 6.11.1 (Python 3.14), and valid on 6.11.2. New tests: `tests/test_menu_tree.py` (5), `tests/test_plugin_menu.py` (6), one each in `tests/test_main_window_menu_tooltips.py` (replacing the `_safe_submenus_of` test) and `tests/test_plugin_menu_placement.py`.
- **Files**: `Imervue/gui/menu_tree.py`, `Imervue/Imervue_main_window.py`, `Imervue/gui/command_palette.py`, `Imervue/paint/shortcuts_dialog.py`, `Imervue/menu/plugin_menu.py`, `Imervue/menu/language_menu.py`, `Imervue/integration_guide.py`, `plugins/safety_review/safety_review.py`, the four test files, `PLUGIN_DEV_GUIDE.md`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.
- **Open items**: `progress.md` #16 (move the PySide6 pin to 6.11.2), #17 (mirror safety_review 1.0.1 to Imervue_Plugins).

## U-20260922-17 · 2026-09-22 · Face detection on OpenCV 5; keep plugin installs on OpenCV 4 · #done #opencv #plugins

- **What**: `tests/test_face_detection.py` failed twice in the local `.venv` because `cv2` resolved to `opencv-python-headless` 5.0.0.93, which nudenet 3.4.2 (safety_review) pulled in unpinned next to `opencv-python` and `opencv-contrib-python` 4.13.0.92; all three write the one shared `cv2` directory. OpenCV 5 moved `CascadeClassifier` to contrib (`xobjdetect`) and no 5.0 wheel ships the Haar XML files: in `opencv-contrib-python` 5.0.0.93 the class exists but `cv2/data/` holds only `__init__.py`. Closes `progress.md` #18.
- **Fix**: `face_detection._load_cascade` raises `FaceDetectorUnavailableError` (a `RuntimeError`) when the class or the cascade file is missing; the dialog shows the new `face_needs_opencv4` string (five languages) with the install command. The plugin installer runs every `pip install` with `-c` and a constraints file from the new `Imervue/plugin/pip_constraints.py` that keeps `opencv-python`, `-headless`, `-contrib` and `-contrib-headless` below 5, so a plugin install no longer mixes major versions in `cv2`. README tables list `opencv-python<5`; `docs/*/index.rst`, `architecture.md` and `PLUGIN_DEV_GUIDE.md` say why.
- **Venv**: `opencv-python-headless` reinstalled at 4.13.0.92, then `opencv-contrib-python` 4.13.0.92 force-reinstalled last so `cv2` is the contrib superset; `pip check` is clean.
- **Result / numbers**: `pip install --dry-run --ignore-installed opencv-python-headless` resolves 5.0.0.93 without the constraints file and 4.14.0.94 with it. New tests: `tests/test_face_detection.py` (+10, and the two Haar tests skip instead of fail where OpenCV has no cascade), `tests/test_face_detection_dialog.py` (5), `tests/test_pip_constraints.py` (6), `tests/test_pip_installer.py` (8). Full suite (`py -m pytest tests/`): 11,071 passed, 1 skipped (reportlab not installed).
- **Files**: `Imervue/image/face_detection.py`, `Imervue/gui/face_detection_dialog.py`, `Imervue/plugin/pip_constraints.py`, `Imervue/plugin/pip_installer.py`, `Imervue/multi_language/{english,traditional_chinese,chinese,japanese,korean}.py`, the four test files, `README.md`, `README/README_*.md`, `docs/*/index.rst`, `architecture.md`, `architecture_explore.md`, `PLUGIN_DEV_GUIDE.md`, `progress.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-01 · 2026-09-23 · Clear the 153 Sphinx warnings and build the docs with -W in CI · #done #docs #ci

- **What**: the 153 warnings were 138 inline-markup errors in ja, ko, zh-cn and zh-tw, 6 CJK title underlines shorter than the title's display width, 8 duplicate `autosectionlabel` labels and one missing `_static` directory. Inline markup must be followed by whitespace or closing punctuation and preceded by whitespace or opening punctuation, which CJK letters and the full-width `（` are not, so each such span got an escaped space (`\ `) on the offending side (231 insertions, never inside code blocks). Literals nested inside strong text (magenta `#FF00FF` in eight languages, `live2d-py` in ja and ko) became plain text inside the strong span, since RST does not nest markup. Underlines were lengthened to the display width (full-width characters count 2). The second "Animation" heading in eight languages, the animated-image keys under the keyboard shortcut reference, became "Animated Images" (zh-cn and zh-tw already said 動畫播放). `docs/conf.py` no longer names the missing `_static` and `_templates` directories. Closes `progress.md` #15.
- **CI**: `test.yml` has a `docs` job that installs `docs/requirements.txt` and runs `sphinx -W --keep-going`, so a warning fails the build. `tests/test_docs_conf.py` checks that `templates_path` and `html_static_path` entries exist.
- **Result / numbers**: `py -m sphinx -W --keep-going -b html docs <out>` exits 0 with no warnings. Before, the rendered ja, ko, zh-cn and zh-tw pages showed 22–58 raw ` `` ` and up to 102 raw `**` each; now none of the ten pages shows raw ` `` `, `**` or `\ ` outside code blocks.
- **Files**: `docs/*/index.rst`, `docs/conf.py`, `.github/workflows/test.yml`, `tests/test_docs_conf.py`, `architecture.md`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-02 · 2026-09-23 · Remove the unused plugin manifest module · #done #plugins #cleanup

- **What**: `Imervue/plugin/plugin_manifest.py` (version-constraint parsing, a `PluginManifest` read from `plugin_requires_imervue`, `plugin_requires_packages` and `plugin_api_version`, and a compatibility check) was imported only by its own test. `plugin_manager` never called it, no plugin here or in Imervue_Plugins declares those attributes, and no other repository in the workspace imports it. Wiring it in was rejected: the main program has no runtime version source (no `__version__`, and a frozen build need not carry package metadata), and gating a plugin's load on `plugin_requires_packages` would contradict the documented pattern, in which a plugin loads without its heavy packages and asks `ensure_dependencies` for them on first use. The module and `tests/test_plugin_manifest.py` are deleted. Closes `progress.md` #5.
- **Files**: `Imervue/plugin/plugin_manifest.py`, `tests/test_plugin_manifest.py` (deleted); `architecture.md`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.

## U-20260923-05 · 2026-09-23 · The EXE shipped no plugins: --include-data-dir skips .py files · #incident #packaging #plugins

- **Symptom**: the release build (U-20260923-03) printed `Nuitka-Options:WARNING: No data files in directory 'plugins'` and `Imervue.dist` had no `plugins/` directory at all, although `release.yml` and `nuitka.md` both say the whole plugin directory is mirrored into the bundle and `plugin_manager` loads `<app dir>/plugins` from disk. PyInstaller (`Imervue.spec`) has always copied it.
- **Cause**: `--include-data-dir` copies "all non-code files" — every tracked plugin file is a `.py` (62 of them, 17 plugins), so the flag matched nothing. The three-value form of `--include-data-files` takes a pattern and does copy them: verified on a throwaway project that `--include-data-files=plugins=plugins/=**/*.py` ships `plugins/demo/__init__.py` and `plugins/demo/demo.py` while skipping `__pycache__`, `models/w.onnx` and `notes.txt`.
- **Fix**: the flag changes in `release.yml` and in all five command listings in `nuitka.md`, whose §2.4 note now says why the data-dir form must not come back. `tests/test_release_workflow.py` requires the new flag, fails if the old one reappears, and checks `nuitka.md` documents the same flag.
- **Result / numbers**: a rebuild with the new flag (same pinned 3.12 environment, Nuitka 4.1.3, detached worktree) ships all 17 plugin directories, 62 `.py` files, 0 `.pyc` and 0 model weights; `Imervue.dist` grew from 686 MB to 687 MB, and the EXE still starts with its main window responding.
- **Files**: `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `nuitka.md`, `tests/test_release_workflow.py`.
- **Gates**: 61 workflow tests pass; the full suite reported one failure, `tests/test_apply_save_worker.py::test_finalize_worker_waits_for_a_real_thread_before_clearing`, which reproduces on `59af060` (before any of today's code changes) and is fixed in U-20260923-06.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-06 · 2026-09-23 · A fixed processEvents count made a worker test fail 4 runs in 5 · #incident #tests

- **Symptom**: `tests/test_apply_save_worker.py::test_finalize_worker_waits_for_a_real_thread_before_clearing` failed in a full run and then in 4 of 5 isolated runs, while earlier full runs the same day passed. It reproduces on `59af060`, before any of the day's code changes, so it is the test, not the code.
- **Cause**: the test started a real `QThread` and pumped `qapp.processEvents()` exactly 50 times waiting for the queued `done` → `finalize_worker` delivery. How many passes that needs depends on machine load — with a Nuitka build and an EXE running alongside, 50 was not enough. It also left the thread running when the assertion failed, which can abort the process.
- **Fix**: a `pump_until(predicate, timeout=5.0)` fixture in `tests/conftest.py` pumps until the predicate holds or the timeout passes and returns the final value; the test uses it and joins the thread in a `finally`. `tests/test_ai_object_remove.py::test_dialog_smoke` had the same 20-pass loop and now uses the fixture too.
- **Result / numbers**: the previously failing test passed 6 runs out of 6 after the change (it failed 4 of 5 before). `tests/test_conftest_pump_until.py` covers the fixture: immediate hit, timeout, a predicate that flips later, and a real queued `QTimer` event.
- **Files**: `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_apply_save_worker.py`, `tests/test_ai_object_remove.py`, `tests/test_conftest_pump_until.py`, `CLAUDE.md` (the rule: no fixed `processEvents()` counts, join real threads in a `finally`).
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-07 · 2026-09-23 · The packaged EXE wrote no log; set it up before Qt is imported · #incident #packaging #logging

- **Symptom**: the EXE built in U-20260923-03 left no `imervue.log` next to it. `setup_logging()` was only called from `exe/start_Imervue.py`, a shim the bundle ships as data but never runs: the EXE is built with `--python-flag=-m ... Imervue`, so it starts `Imervue/__main__.py`, which never configured logging or an excepthook. With `--windows-console-mode=disable` there is no stderr either, so a crash in a released build left nothing behind.
- **Fix**: `Imervue/__main__.py` gained a `main()` that calls `setup_logging()` and `install_exception_logging()` first and imports PySide6 afterwards, so even an import-time failure is logged (module-level imports would have tripped E402 and could not run after a statement). `log_setup` grew `install_exception_logging()` — the hook the shim used to define, now shared — plus three fixes: `setup_logging()` returns early when a file handler is already attached (it is called from both entry points), the stderr handler is decided by `app_paths.is_frozen()` instead of `sys.frozen` (Nuitka sets only `__compiled__`, so a windowed build was attaching a handler to a stderr that does not exist), and a log directory that cannot be written — an EXE installed under `Program Files` — falls back to `%LOCALAPPDATA%/Imervue` (`~/.cache/imervue` elsewhere).
- **Result / numbers**: `tests/test_log_setup.py` grows to 11 tests (idempotence, the read-only fallback, directory creation, both `_user_log_dir` branches, the excepthook logging and chaining) and the new `tests/test_main_entry.py` (5) asserts `main()` calls the two setup functions before `parse_args()` and that no module-level line in the entry point imports PySide6.
- **Files**: `Imervue/__main__.py`, `Imervue/system/log_setup.py`, `exe/start_Imervue.py`, `tests/test_log_setup.py`, `tests/test_main_entry.py`, `architecture.md`, `architecture_explore.md`, `PLUGIN_DEV_GUIDE.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-09 · 2026-09-23 · 10 of 17 plugins failed to load in the EXE: compile every Imervue submodule · #incident #packaging #plugins

- **Symptom**: with plugins finally shipped (U-20260923-05) and the entry point writing a log (U-20260923-07), the first run of the EXE showed `imervue.log` full of `Failed to load plugin package 'ai_colorize': No module named 'Imervue.plugin.model_dir'` — 10 of the 17 bundled plugins failed; the other 7 loaded. Missing modules: `Imervue.plugin.model_dir` (7 plugins), `Imervue.image.inpaint` (2), `Imervue.plugin.subprocess_util` (1).
- **Cause**: Nuitka follows imports from the entry point; it does not pull in a package wholesale, even when the package is the compilation target. Plugins are loaded from disk at run time by `plugin_manager`, so nothing in the compiled graph reaches the modules only they import. `nuitka.md` §2.4 claimed the opposite ("Nuitka walks the whole package automatically") and told readers not to use `--include-package=Imervue`; that section is now rewritten with this evidence. The PyInstaller specs had the same hole: `collect_submodules` was called for PySide6 only.
- **Fix**: `--include-package=Imervue` in `release.yml` and every `nuitka.md` listing; `hiddenimports += collect_submodules('Imervue')` in `Imervue.spec` and `Imervue_mac.spec`; `--collect-submodules Imervue` in the `pyinstaller.md` listings, with the reason and a troubleshooting row in both documents. `tests/test_release_workflow.py` requires the flag and the spec call.
- **Result / numbers**: rebuilt with the same pinned environment: 17 plugins loaded, 0 failed, no ERROR line in the log, and the bundle grew from 687 MB to 689 MB. Nuitka prints `Not allowed to include module 'Imervue.__main__' due to 'Main program is already included in package mode.'` — expected in package mode and documented as such.
- **Files**: `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `nuitka.md`, `pyinstaller.md`, `Imervue.spec`, `Imervue_mac.spec`, `tests/test_release_workflow.py`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-11 · 2026-09-23 · Paint workspace tests waited a fixed time after show() · #incident #tests #paint

- **Symptom**: one full run lost four `tests/test_paint_workspace.py` tests (zoom slider sync, workspace inside a tab widget, real mouse click, layer dock `+` button); they passed in isolation and in the next full run.
- **Cause**: each one `show()`ed a widget, slept a fixed `QTest.qWait(20..100)` and then asserted — on the window being exposed, the canvas layout having converged after its first fit, and a queued repaint or signal having arrived. How long that takes depends on machine load. It was the only test file still using `qWait`.
- **Fix**: `QTest.qWaitForWindowExposed()` after every `show()`, then `pump_until` (U-20260923-06) on the condition itself: the slider reading 150, the widget centre mapping inside the document, the fit having raised the zoom above `ZOOM_MIN`, the pixels changing after the click, the layer count going up. No `qWait` sleep is left in the test suite.
- **Result / numbers**: 21 of 21 pass three times alone and five times in a row while a full suite runs alongside.
- **Files**: `tests/test_paint_workspace.py`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-14 · 2026-09-23 · Clipboard tests use an in-process fake, not the OS clipboard · #incident #tests

- **Symptom**: `test_swatch_panel_context_menu.py::test_copy_hex_lands_on_clipboard` failed in the U-20260923-13 full run and passed 3 of 3 isolated — the third clipboard test to flake this way (`CLAUDE.md` listed the other two as a known trap to re-run around).
- **Cause**: all three used the real OS clipboard, which other processes share: `dataChanged` arrives asynchronously via `WM_CLIPBOARDUPDATE`, so two quick sets can coalesce into one signal, and a set silently fails while another program holds the clipboard open. They also overwrote the developer's clipboard: with a sentinel string on it, the old tests left `#FF0000` there.
- **Fix**: a `fake_clipboard` fixture in `tests/conftest.py` patches `QApplication.clipboard()` with an in-process `QObject` holding one `QMimeData` (text, image, mime data, clear) that emits `dataChanged` synchronously on every change. `tests/test_clipboard_monitor.py`, `tests/test_image_list_view.py` and `tests/test_swatch_panel_context_menu.py` use it. Every clipboard access in `Imervue/` and `plugins/` already goes through `QApplication.clipboard()`, so the product code is exercised unchanged. `CLAUDE.md` replaces the "clipboard tests flake, re-run them" note with the rule to take the fixture.
- **Result / numbers**: the 44 tests of the three files pass three times in a row, and the sentinel on the real clipboard is still there afterwards. `tests/test_conftest_fake_clipboard.py` (8) covers the fake: it is what `QApplication.clipboard()` returns, starts empty, round-trips text and images, emits once per change, clears, accepts `setMimeData` and mode arguments.
- **Files**: `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_conftest_fake_clipboard.py`, `tests/test_clipboard_monitor.py`, `tests/test_image_list_view.py`, `tests/test_swatch_panel_context_menu.py`, `CLAUDE.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

Supporting moves, also behaviour-free: the kind / tool name constants become public in `annotation_models.py` (`KIND_*`, `TOOL_*`; the unused `_KIND_PEN` is gone); the handle size becomes `annotation_drawing.HANDLE_SIZE` and the handle cursor `annotation_crop.handle_cursor`; and `pil_to_qimage` / `qimage_to_pil` move to the new `system/qimage_convert.py`, which `clipboard_monitor.py` now uses in place of its own byte-for-byte copy `_qimage_to_pil`. `annotation_dialog.py` keeps re-exporting everything older call sites import from it. One step of `progress.md` #4, now 4 files.
- **Result / numbers**: the canvas was rendered before and after the move with six shape / text annotations, all nine freehand brushes, a mosaic and a blur region — plain, with a selection, and in crop mode — and the three grabs are pixel-identical once annotation ids and `PYTHONHASHSEED` are fixed. The removed-name check finds no caller left on a moved name. The converter tests move to `tests/test_qimage_convert.py`, which adds promotion, buffer ownership, a both-way round trip and grayscale. Annotation / Modify / clipboard tests: 279 passed.
- **Found on the way**: the spray, charcoal and crayon jitter is seeded with `hash(ann.id)`, and Python randomises `str` hashes per process, so the same saved project looks different every time it is reopened (without the fixed hash seed, two runs of the same code differ in 6,658 pixels). Fixed in the next entry.
- **Files**: `Imervue/gui/{annotation_canvas,annotation_drawing,annotation_crop,annotation_destructive,annotation_models,annotation_dialog}.py`, `Imervue/system/{qimage_convert,clipboard_monitor}.py`, `tests/test_qimage_convert.py`, `tests/test_clipboard_monitor.py`, `architecture_explore.md`, `architecture.md`, `progress.md`.
- **Open items**: `progress.md` #4.

## U-20260923-19 · 2026-09-23 · Brush jitter changed on every reopen: seed it with CRC32, not hash() · #incident #annotation

- **Symptom**: found while pixel-comparing the annotation canvas for U-20260923-18 — two runs of the same code on the same annotations differed in 6,658 pixels, all inside the spray, charcoal and crayon strokes. A saved annotation project therefore looked different every time it was reopened, and the baked PNG did not match what had been on screen in an earlier session.
- **Cause**: those brushes scatter their marks from `random.Random(hash(ann.id) & 0xFFFFFFFF)`, in four places in the Qt preview (`annotation_drawing.py`) and four in the PIL bake (`annotation_models.py`). The intent (the bake comment says "seeded RNG so repeated bakes of the same annotation match") holds only inside one process: Python randomises `str` hashes per process unless `PYTHONHASHSEED` is set.
- **Fix**: `annotation_models.jitter_seed(annotation_id)` returns `zlib.crc32` of the id, and all eight sites use it.
- **Result / numbers**: `tests/test_annotation_jitter_seed.py` (6) bakes spray, charcoal, crayon and pencil strokes in subprocesses with different `PYTHONHASHSEED` values and requires identical output, and in-process output equal to a subprocess's; three of its tests failed before the fix. Two canvas renders with `PYTHONHASHSEED=random` now differ in 0 pixels (6,658 before).
- **Files**: `Imervue/gui/annotation_models.py`, `Imervue/gui/annotation_drawing.py`, `tests/test_annotation_jitter_seed.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-37 · 2026-09-23 · Keep tests off the OS Recycle Bin with an autouse os_trash fixture · #done #tests #flaky

- **What**: every delete test went through `send2trash`, a shell operation on the Windows Recycle Bin that every other process shares. `tests/test_culling_dialog.py::test_delete_rejects_removes_files_and_reloads_the_grid` failed about one run in three even in isolation (`Batch delete failed on: …shot_1.png`), and each suite run filled the developer's own Recycle Bin. The new autouse fixture `os_trash` in `tests/conftest.py` replaces `send2trash.send2trash` with an in-process trash: it takes a path, a path-like or a list, removes each file or directory tree, raises `FileNotFoundError` for a missing path, and yields the list of trashed paths. Product code imports `send2trash` at call time (`system/trash_ops.py`, `keyboard_actions._send_to_trash`), so the patch reaches both; when the package is missing, a stand-in module takes its place so the native `SHFileOperation` fallback is not reached either. `tests/test_conftest_os_trash.py` covers the fixture: single path, list, path-like, directory tree, a missing path stopping the batch, an empty batch, `trash_batch` and `_send_to_trash` going through it, and the stand-in module. `CLAUDE.md` gains the rule next to the clipboard one. The map's per-section file / line counts for `gui/`, `paint/`, `puppet/`, `desktop_pet/` and `tests/` were stale and are regenerated, and its shared-fixture list now names `pump_until`, `fake_clipboard` and `os_trash`.
- **Result / numbers**: the culling test passed 6 of 6 isolated runs (was 2 of 3). Full suite 11,266 passed, 1 skipped, in 151 s against 228 s before the fake; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_conftest_os_trash.py`, `CLAUDE.md`, `architecture_explore.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-40 · 2026-09-23 · Fix the culling-dialog test race: a worker ran twice · #done #tests #flaky

- **What**: `tests/test_culling_dialog.py` drives the delete worker inline (`_drain` calls `worker.run()`), but `CullingDialog._start_reject_delete` had already called `worker.start()`, so the real thread and the inline call deleted the same files. Whichever lost the race logged `Batch delete failed` and reported the path as failed, failing `test_delete_rejects_removes_files_and_reloads_the_grid` or `test_delete_rejects_clears_the_index_rows` about one run in three. U-20260923-37 blamed the OS Recycle Bin for that failure; the failure persisted with the fake bin in place, and the double run is the cause. A new autouse fixture in the test module patches `FilePurgeWorker.start` to a no-op so only the inline run executes. The other tests that fetch a dialog's worker either join it with `wait()` or build their own, so none repeat the pattern. `CLAUDE.md` gains the rule (patch `start` when running a worker inline) and its `os_trash` bullet no longer claims the bin caused this flake.
- **Result / numbers**: the culling tests passed 12 of 12 isolated runs. Full suite 11,290 passed, 1 skipped.
- **Files**: `tests/test_culling_dialog.py`, `CLAUDE.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-54 · 2026-09-23 · Stop the plugin installer's Python probe from rejecting slow interpreters silently · #done #bug #plugin

- **What**: `Imervue/plugin/pip_installer.py:_verify_python` runs `python -m pip --version` to decide whether an interpreter can install plugin dependencies. It allowed 10 s and returned `False` from a bare `except Exception`, so on a loaded machine a working Python was rejected with no trace. The full suite caught it: `tests/test_plugin.py::TestVerifyPython::test_verify_current_python` failed on the running interpreter during a run slowed by other sessions' suites, and passed in isolation. The probe now allows `_VERIFY_TIMEOUT_S = 30.0` (pip must import before it answers). It catches only `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` (logged as a warning), `OSError` and `ValueError` (a missing, non-executable or malformed path, logged), logs a non-zero pip exit, and lets anything else propagate as a bug. `tests/test_pip_installer.py` gained regression tests, red before the fix: success, pip missing, timeout, missing file, permission error and malformed path, each checking the exact command, the timeout passed, and that a rejection logs the path. Further cases check the 30 s floor and that an unexpected error is no longer swallowed.
- **Result / numbers**: 8 new tests, all failing before and passing after. Full suite 11,392 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/plugin/pip_installer.py`, `tests/test_pip_installer.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-55 · 2026-09-23 · Narrow the blind excepts in image/ (BLE001 batch 1) · #refactor #error-handling #image

- **What**: the `_verify_python` fix (U-20260923-54) prompted a scan. `ruff --select BLE` finds 35 handlers in `Imervue/` that catch `Exception` without narrowing the type or logging the traceback, breaking the workspace rule to catch the most specific exception and never swallow one silently. This batch clears the 10 in `image/`. `recipe_store.py` catches `_RECIPE_DECODE_ERRORS` (`TypeError`, `ValueError`, `IndexError`, everything `Recipe.from_dict` raises on malformed data) in its three decode paths, each logging at debug with the traceback. `thumbnail_disk_cache.py` catches `OSError` / `ValueError` / `DecompressionBombError` reading a cache PNG, `AttributeError` / `IndexError` / `TypeError` / `ValueError` interpreting a non-image array, and `OSError` / `ValueError` writing. In `info.py`, the header read catches PIL's open errors and the ctime conversion `OSError` / `OverflowError` / `ValueError`. Two handlers stay broad with a justified `# noqa: BLE001` and a traceback log: the image-info builder, which shows any decoder failure in the dialog, and the EXIF reader, whose PIL parser fails in open-ended ways; the EXIF reader also no longer uses `AttributeError` as its no-EXIF path for PNGs. The remaining 25 are `progress.md` #21. Tests: undecodable stored recipes and variants (each error type) are dropped while an unexpected error propagates; non-image arrays and write failures are skipped while an unexpected write error propagates; a PNG's EXIF read logs no traceback; an out-of-range ctime becomes `None`.
- **Result / numbers**: `BLE001` findings 35 → 25. 16 new tests; the two "unexpected error propagates" cases fail on the old code. Full suite 11,407 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/image/info.py`, `Imervue/image/recipe_store.py`, `Imervue/image/thumbnail_disk_cache.py`, `tests/test_image_info.py`, `tests/test_recipe_store.py`, `tests/test_thumbnail_disk_cache.py`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.

## U-20260923-56 · 2026-09-23 · Split expected from unexpected failures in plugin/ (BLE001 batch 2) · #refactor #error-handling #plugin

- **What**: `progress.md` #21 continued with the four broad handlers in `plugin/`. Three sit at worker boundaries: `_InstallWorker._install_all`, `FetchPluginListWorker.run` and `DownloadPluginWorker.run`. Each dialog waits on the worker's signal, so the house rule from `gui/_apply_save.py` applies: always report, even a bug. Each now catches the failures it expects first and reports them as before with no traceback. For the installer those are `OSError`, `ValueError` and `subprocess.SubprocessError`. The downloader uses the new `_EXPECTED_FETCH_ERRORS`: network, HTTP and disk errors, bad JSON or a truncated listing, and a listing of the wrong shape. A trailing `except Exception` still reports anything else, and now also logs it with `logger.exception`; before, a bug here left only its message in the dialog. `check_missing_packages` keeps its broad handler with a justified `# noqa: BLE001`, because importing an optional package runs its native init, and any failure there means "missing". Tests: every expected error type is reported without a traceback; an unexpected one is reported and logged once with the traceback (install, fetch, download); a download failure still removes the partial directory; an import raising `RuntimeError` counts as missing.
- **Result / numbers**: `BLE001` findings 25 → 21. 13 new tests; the three traceback tests fail on the old code. Full suite 11,423 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/plugin/pip_installer.py`, `Imervue/plugin/plugin_downloader.py`, `tests/test_pip_installer.py`, `tests/test_plugin_downloader.py`, `tests/test_plugin_downloader_partial.py`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.

## U-20260923-57 · 2026-09-23 · Narrow read_json's blind except (BLE001 batch 3) · #refactor #error-handling #user_settings

- **What**: `progress.md` #21 continued with `user_settings/user_setting_dict.py:read_json`, which every settings and profile load goes through. It caught `Exception` and returned `None`, so a bug while reading settings looked the same as a missing file, and the app quietly started on defaults. It now catches only what a bad file can cause: `OSError` for I/O, `ValueError` for invalid UTF-8 or JSON, and `RecursionError` for nesting too deep to parse. ## U-20260923-58 · 2026-09-23 · PDF exports report an unwritable target instead of claiming success · #bug #export

- **What**: while narrowing `menu/tip_menu.py`'s handlers (#21), the Help menu's cheat-sheet export turned out to report "Cheat sheet saved to …" for a path that could not be written: a missing directory, a directory at that path, or a PDF held open by a viewer. `QPdfWriter` never raises; an unwritable target only makes `QPainter.begin` return `False`, after which every paint call is a no-op and `generate_cheat_sheet` returned the path as usual. `export/contact_sheet.py:generate_contact_sheet` opened its painter the same way, so the contact-sheet dialog also reported success with no file written. The new `export/pdf_output.py:begin_pdf_painter` begins the painter explicitly and raises `OSError` naming the target when that fails. Both generators use it, and their callers already turn an `OSError` into the error toast or the dialog's error message. A search for the related trap, an ignored `QImage.save` / `QPixmap.save` return value, found no other cases.
- **Result / numbers**: 11 new tests. `test_pdf_output.py` checks that a writable target paints and that a missing directory or a directory at the path raises. Both generators raise for an unwritable target and leave nothing behind. The new `test_tip_menu.py` covers the cheat-sheet menu command: saved, unwritable (error toast) and cancelled. The new `test_contact_sheet_dialog.py` covers the render worker's report: success, an unwritable target and no images. The generator and menu regression tests fail on the old code. Full suite 11,434 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/export/pdf_output.py` (new), `Imervue/export/cheat_sheet.py`, `Imervue/export/contact_sheet.py`, `tests/test_pdf_output.py`, `tests/test_cheat_sheet.py`, `tests/test_contact_sheet.py`, `tests/test_contact_sheet_dialog.py`, `tests/test_tip_menu.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260923-59 · 2026-09-23 · Narrow the blind excepts in menu/; share IMAGE_READ_ERRORS (BLE001 batch 4) · #refactor #error-handling #menu #image

- **What**: `progress.md` #21 continued with the three handlers in `menu/`. Reading an image to sort or bucket it is repeated across the tree, so what Pillow raises is now one named constant: `image/read_errors.py:IMAGE_READ_ERRORS`. It holds `OSError` (a missing or unreadable file, `UnidentifiedImageError`, truncated or corrupt data), `ValueError`, and `DecompressionBombError`, which is not an `OSError`. Before settling on it, 10,500 corrupted PNG, JPEG, GIF, TIFF, WebP, BMP and ICO files (random byte flips and truncation, then open, thumbnail and convert) were decoded, and nothing outside the tuple was raised. `sort_menu._sort_key_resolution` catches it. `image/info.py` and `image/thumbnail_disk_cache.py`, which spelled the same tuple out in batch 1, now use the constant. In `tip_menu.py`, the support-bundle and cheat-sheet commands catch `OSError` / `ValueError`, which covers every failure U-20260923-58 made them report. Anything else now reaches the global exception hook instead of a toast that hid its type. The architecture map's `image/` intro claimed no module imports Qt except for `QPainter` text rasterising. In fact only `info.py` imports Qt (`QMessageBox`), so the intro now says that, and its file and line counts are regenerated with the rest of the map.
- **Result / numbers**: `BLE001` findings 20 → 17. 16 new tests: `test_read_errors.py` (a seeded corruption sweep per format, missing and non-image files, a decompression bomb), `test_sort_menu.py` (pixel count; missing, non-image and over-limit files sort as 0; an unexpected error propagates) and the report and cheat-sheet cases in `test_tip_menu.py`. Full suite 11,451 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.

## U-20260923-60 · 2026-09-23 · EXIF editor: saving works again and User Comment round-trips · #bug #metadata #gui

- **What**: the first test written for `gui/exif_editor.py` (while narrowing its handler for #21) found that Save could never succeed. `_save` encodes the User Comment with `piexif.helper.UserComment.dump`, but `piexif.helper` is a submodule that `import piexif` does not load, and nothing in the tree imported it. Every save therefore raised `AttributeError` before writing anything. The error left the Qt slot for the global hook, so the dialog stayed open with no message. Two more defects sat behind it. `UserComment.dump` defaults to the ASCII character code, so any non-ASCII comment (Chinese, accented letters) was saved as `?`. And the field was prefilled by decoding the raw value as UTF-8, so it showed the 8-byte character-code prefix (`ASCII\0\0\0…`), and every re-save wrapped the comment in another prefix. The editor now imports `piexif.helper` next to `piexif`. It saves ASCII text with the ASCII code and anything else with UNICODE. It prefills through the new `_decode_user_comment`, which strips the prefix and falls back to UTF-8 for a value without a recognised prefix.
- **Result / numbers**: new `tests/test_exif_editor.py`, 8 tests. They cover: a save writes the tag, then toasts, refreshes the sidebar and accepts; a non-ASCII comment is written intact; an ASCII comment keeps the ASCII code; a saved comment reopens without its prefix (ASCII and CJK); `_decode_user_comment` handles UNICODE, unprefixed and empty values. Every save test fails on the old code. Full suite 11,488 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/gui/exif_editor.py`, `tests/test_exif_editor.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260923-63 · 2026-09-23 · Enable ruff's blind-except rules (closes #21) · #done #tooling #error-handling

- **What**: with every broad `except Exception` in `Imervue/` narrowed or logged (U-20260923-55 to -62), `BLE` joins `[tool.ruff.lint] select` in `pyproject.toml`, so a new blind except now fails `ruff check .`. Enabling it exposed two kinds of leftover. 28 `# noqa: BLE001` comments sat on handlers that already log with `logger.exception`, which BLE accepts. They suppressed nothing, so their reasons became plain comments (bare ones were dropped), and `ruff check . --extend-select RUF100` now reports no unused `BLE001`. And `tests/test_paint_canvas.py:_make_canvas`, which returns `None` when the GL widget cannot be built headless, gets a justified `# noqa: BLE001`. `CLAUDE.md` lists `BLE` among the enforced rule sets. The architecture map gains §10.11, the house conventions for exception handling: `IMAGE_READ_ERRORS` for image reads, expected-then-logged-unexpected at worker boundaries, and a justified noqa with a traceback log only where a third party's failure types are open-ended.
- **Result / numbers**: ruff with `BLE` selected: 0 findings (was 35 at the start of #21). Full suite 11,515 passed, 1 skipped; bandit clean.

## U-20260923-64 · 2026-09-23 · Pasted and fallback-trashed files no longer overwrite each other · #bug #gpu_image_view #data-loss

- **What**: a sweep for the silent-failure class behind U-20260923-58 (Qt calls that report failure only through a return value) found `gpu_image_view/clipboard_paste.py:_save_clipboard_image`. It ignored `QImage.save`'s result. On a read-only folder the paste still added a path that did not exist to the image list, tried to open it, and toasted "Pasted: …". The same function named the file `pasted_<seconds>.png`, so a second paste within the same second silently overwrote the first. It now tries `-1`, `-2`, … until the name is free, and returns `None` when the save fails; the caller then toasts an error naming the folder and leaves the list untouched. A search for other second-resolution names found the same collision in `keyboard_actions._send_to_trash`'s fallback for a machine without send2trash. When a same-named file was already in the trash it added a timestamp suffix, so a third file of that name trashed within one second overwrote the second. The new `_free_trash_name` picks the first free `stem_N.ext`. For the freedesktop trash it also skips names that already have a `.trashinfo`, as the spec requires. `system/error_report.py` already had its own free-name loop.
- **Result / numbers**: 6 new tests. `test_clipboard_paste.py` (new): a paste saves, lists, opens and toasts; two pastes in one second keep both files; a failed save toasts an error and neither opens nor lists anything. `test_keyboard_actions.py`: three same-named files trashed on Linux keep all three copies and their `.trashinfo` files, the same on macOS, and a stale `.trashinfo` is skipped. The paste and three-copy tests fail on the old code. Full suite 11,521 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/gpu_image_view/clipboard_paste.py`, `Imervue/gpu_image_view/actions/keyboard_actions.py`, `tests/test_clipboard_paste.py`, `tests/test_keyboard_actions.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

It pins the top-level order, title and minimum size, the source-count label, and each settings row: label, spin type, range, default, step, suffix and tooltip. It also covers the Export button calling `_export` with the resolved images, Close hiding the dialog, and `_resolve_images` preferring the selection over the folder. The map row for `menu/filter_menu.py` still described only extension and rating filters; it now lists what the Filter menu offers.

## U-20260923-68 · 2026-09-23 · Multi-selection exports and renames follow the view order · #bug #gpu_image_view #gui

- **What**: while splitting `SlideshowMp4Dialog` (U-20260923-67), its `_resolve_images` turned out to be copied verbatim into `ContactSheetDialog` and `WebGalleryDialog`, and all three returned `[p for p in selected_tiles]`. `GPUImageView.selected_tiles` is a `set`, so a multi-selection came back in hash order, which also changes from run to run because string hashing is randomised. A slideshow MP4, contact sheet PDF or web gallery made from selected images was therefore shuffled. The same `list(selected_tiles)` fed four more order-sensitive entry points. Batch rename and token rename number files in list order, the GIF / video dialog uses it as the initial frame order, and the collage uses it as the layout order. `gpu_image_view/actions/select.py` gains `selected_in_view_order(viewer)`, which lists the selected paths in the viewer's image order, then any selected path the model no longer lists, sorted. It also gains `selection_or_all(viewer)`, which returns that list or every image when nothing is selected (`[]` without a viewer). The three export dialogs' `_resolve_images` are now one line each, and `open_batch_rename`, `open_batch_move`, `open_token_rename`, `open_gif_video_dialog` and `open_collage` use the helpers. Callers where order does not matter (batch export, culling, tagging, staging) are unchanged.
- **Result / numbers**: new `tests/test_select.py`, 15 tests: view order over 24 synthetic paths (the test first checks that hash order really differs), paths missing from the model, non-string entries, the fallback to every image, and each of the eight entry points receiving the selection in view order. The entry-point tests fail on the old code. Full suite 11,549 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/gpu_image_view/actions/select.py`, `Imervue/gpu_image_view/actions/batch_ops.py`, `Imervue/gui/slideshow_mp4_dialog.py`, `Imervue/gui/contact_sheet_dialog.py`, `Imervue/gui/web_gallery_dialog.py`, `Imervue/gui/token_rename_dialog.py`, `Imervue/gui/gif_video_dialog.py`, `Imervue/gui/collage_dialog.py`, `tests/test_select.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260923-69 · 2026-09-23 · Combine-to-PDF/TIFF pages and staging-tray adds follow the view order · #bug #menu

- **What**: a follow-up sweep of every remaining `list(selected_tiles)` after U-20260923-68. Two more were order-sensitive, both in `menu/right_click_menu.py`. `_combine_multipage` builds a multi-page PDF or TIFF from the selection, so its pages came out in hash order. `_add_to_staging_tray` appended the selection to the tray in hash order. Both now take `selected_in_view_order`. The rest were checked and left alone because their result does not depend on order: batch export (its counter only avoids collisions), batch rotate, tagging, location and keyword import, the auto-cull passes, auto-orient, import by date, events, AI upscale and hierarchical tags. The plugin uses are either a single-item check (`video_source`) or an upload list (`cloud_share`), where order does not matter either.

## U-20260923-70 · 2026-09-23 · Guard against modules no production code imports; 58 found · #tooling #architecture

- **What**: `export/gallery_sort.py`, written in June as the web gallery's sort, filter and group step, turned out to be imported only by its own test. That led to a whole-tree scan with the AST: every import in `Imervue/` and `plugins/` (tests excluded), with relative imports resolved, and dotted `Imervue.…` string literals counted for lazy `importlib` loads. It found 61 modules nothing imports. Three are entry points run with `py -m` (`Imervue.__main__`, `Imervue.cli`, `Imervue.mcp_server.__main__`). The other 58 (8,803 lines) have tests but no caller, so no user can reach them: 34 in `paint/` (for example `rich_text.py`, `speech_bubbles.py`, `gradient_editor.py`, `perspective_warp.py`, `magnetic_lasso.py`, `tablet_mapping.py`), 6 in `puppet/` (IK, easing, lip-sync, mesh repair and more), and 18 spread over nine other packages. `paint/watercolor.py`, `paint/comic_formats.py` and `paint/speech_bubbles.py` duplicate implementations that are wired: the watercolor brush in `gui/annotation_models.py`, CBZ / PDF export in `paint/paint_project_export.py`, and `paint/speech_bubble.py`. README's feature claims (watercolor brush, CBZ / PDF comic export, the pet's speech bubble) are all backed by wired code. Whether each module gets a menu or is deleted is a product call, so nothing was removed. The new `tests/test_unwired_modules.py` records the 58 in `_KNOWN_UNWIRED` and fails on any new unimported module; wiring or deleting a listed one fails until its entry is dropped. `progress.md` #22 asks the owner to decide. The architecture map's §12 gains a trap entry: a module's row in the package tables does not mean the feature is reachable.
- **Result / numbers**: 3 new tests (no new unwired module, the known list current, the scan seeing absolute, relative and in-function imports); the scan is cached and takes about 3 s. Full suite 11,554 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `tests/test_unwired_modules.py`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.
- **Open items**: `progress.md` #22.

## U-20260923-74 · 2026-09-23 · Split the main window's closeEvent (#19) · #refactor #gui

- **What**: `progress.md` #19 continued with `Imervue_main_window.py:ImervueMainWindow.closeEvent` (84 lines). The teardown groups moved to `_release_for_close`: folder-session snapshot, tab-signal disconnect, watchdog, tree workers, Modify panel and GL textures. Geometry, settings and pending deletions moved to `_persist_for_close`. `closeEvent` keeps the last-window decision, the plugin unload, and `os._exit`. Every best-effort group and its comments moved unchanged. No test builds the real window, and the last-window path ends in `os._exit`, so the new `tests/test_main_window_close.py` re-binds `closeEvent`'s code to a stand-in class. That makes its zero-argument `super()` land on a recording base, every collaborator records its calls into one log, and `os._exit` is patched to record. The tests were checked against the old code. They pin the full step order for the last window (ending in the plugin hooks, `accept`, `super().closeEvent` and `os._exit(0)`) and for a secondary window (ending in `deleteLater`). They also cover the watchdog step being skipped when there is no watchdog, and a failing step skipping only the rest of its own group. One unrelated flake appeared in the full run: the known webhook e2e `WinError 10053`. That file passes 24 of 24 on its own.
- **Result / numbers**: `closeEvent` 84 → 34 lines; functions over 80 lines 10 → 9. 4 new tests. Full suite 11,564 passed, 1 skipped (plus the flake above); ruff and bandit clean. The module is now 971 lines, close to the 1,000-line limit; the map had it at 924 though it was already 961.

## U-20260923-77 · 2026-09-23 · Move the plugin installer's Python finder into python_finder (#23) · #refactor #plugin

- **What**: `progress.md` #23 continued with `plugin/pip_installer.py` (993 lines). The interpreter search moves to the new `plugin/python_finder.py`: `_find_python` and its Windows registry, install-folder and Unix helpers, the embedded-Python paths, `_verify_python` with `_VERIFY_TIMEOUT_S`, and the `_subprocess_kwargs` they share with the installer. The code moved unchanged. Plugins in Imervue_Plugins (`ai_background_remover`, `object_splitter`, `safety_review`) call `from Imervue.plugin.pip_installer import _find_python`, and tests patch the probe through `pip_installer`. So `pip_installer` imports those names back with a justified `# noqa: F401`. The import path is now a written contract in `architecture.md` §6. The new `tests/test_python_finder.py` covers the running interpreter outside a frozen build, and the frozen search order: a PATH hit wins, and a PATH candidate without pip is skipped. The registry comes before install folders on Windows (Unix paths elsewhere), then the embedded Python, which is rejected without pip. It also checks the subprocess defaults (no stdin, UTF-8 with replacement, no console window on Windows) and that `pip_installer` still exposes each finder name as the same object. The existing installer and plugin tests pass unchanged.
- **Result / numbers**: `pip_installer.py` 993 → 811 lines; `python_finder.py` 218 lines. 7 new tests. Full suite 11,577 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/plugin/pip_installer.py`, `Imervue/plugin/python_finder.py` (new), `tests/test_python_finder.py`, `architecture.md`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.
- **Open items**: `progress.md` #23.

## U-20260923-78 · 2026-09-23 · Stop test_ui_scale's app font leaking into later test files · #tests #flaky

- **What**: `tests/test_duplicate_detection_dialog.py::test_layout_order_and_options_row` failed once in a full `-n 6 --dist loadfile` run and passed on its own. Cause: `tests/test_ui_scale.py` calls `apply_ui_scale` and `QApplication.setFont` several times and never restores the font, so every later file in the same xdist worker builds its widgets with a font of about 72 px. `QHeaderView`'s minimum section size grows with the font, and `setColumnWidth(0, 68)` came back as 107. Running `test_ui_scale.py` before the duplicate-dialog file reproduced it every time. The new `tests/_app_font.py:app_font_restored` snapshots the app font and puts it back on exit (also when the body raises), only when it changed, and never creates a QApplication; the autouse `_restore_app_font` fixture in `tests/conftest.py` wraps every test in it, so no test can leak a font change into another file.
- **Result / numbers**: the reproducing pair now passes; 4 new tests in `tests/test_app_font.py`, one of them the regression (a 72 px app font widens a 68 px column; after the guard exits it is 68 again). Full suite 11,577 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `tests/_app_font.py`, `tests/test_app_font.py`, `tests/conftest.py`, `architecture_explore.md`, `docs/updates/`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260923-79 · 2026-09-23 · Restore the app stylesheet between tests too · #tests #flaky

- **What**: follow-up to U-20260923-78. A theme stylesheet is QApplication state as well: `apply_theme(app, "nord")` shrinks a `QPushButton("Scan")` size hint from 81 to 53 px wide. `tests/test_themes.py` only leaves the default theme behind because its last `apply_theme` test happens to run last; running `test_apply_theme_sets_stylesheet` on its own (or with `--random-order`, installed here) leaves Nord applied for the rest of the worker. The guard now covers both: `tests/_app_font.py` became `tests/_app_appearance.py:app_appearance_restored` (font and stylesheet, each reset only when it changed) and the autouse fixture became `_restore_app_appearance`.
- **Result / numbers**: `tests/test_app_font.py` became `tests/test_app_appearance.py`, 5 tests (was 4), including a stylesheet regression: Nord changes a button's width inside the guard, and after it exits the stylesheet is empty and the width is back. Full suite 11,581 passed, 1 skipped; ruff clean.
- **Files**: `tests/_app_appearance.py`, `tests/test_app_appearance.py`, `tests/conftest.py`, `architecture_explore.md`, `docs/updates/`.

## U-20260923-82 · 2026-09-23 · A hidden desktop pet no longer pops a speech bubble · #bugfix #desktop_pet

- **What**: a pet hidden by its hotkey, the tray menu or hide-on-fullscreen keeps its webhook, Windows-notification and hotkey listeners running, and each of them can call `PetWindow._show_speech`. The bubble is a parentless top-level `SpeechBubble`, so it appeared on its own, anchored to the hidden pet, over the fullscreen app the pet had just hidden for. `hideEvent` only closed a bubble that was already open. `_show_speech` now returns when the pet window is not visible. Found by a one-off probe that recorded every test leaving a visible top-level window: `tests/test_desktop_pet_drop_dispatch.py` left bubbles from a never-shown pet.
- **Result / numbers**: 3 new tests in `tests/test_desktop_pet_window.py`: a shown pet shows its bubble; a pet never shown, or hidden after speaking, shows none; a pet hidden by fullscreen stays silent on a notification. The last two failed before the fix. Full suite 11,585 passed, 1 skipped, plus the known webhook e2e flake (`test_desktop_pet_webhook_server.py` passes 24 of 24 on its own); ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/desktop_pet/pet_window.py`, `tests/test_desktop_pet_window.py`, `docs/updates/`.
- **Open items**: none.

## U-20260924-05 · 2026-09-24 · Webhook 404/401 replies no longer lost to a connection reset (the "flaky" e2e test) · #bug #desktop_pet #tests

- **What**: `tests/test_desktop_pet_webhook_server.py` has long failed now and then with `ConnectionAbortedError: [WinError 10053]` on a loaded machine and on CI, always in `test_e2e_invalid_path_returns_404` or `test_e2e_auth_required_when_token_set`, never in the tests that get a 200 or 400. It was treated as an unexplained flaky socket test, to be re-run. The cause is a real bug in `desktop_pet/webhook_server.py`. `do_POST` answered a wrong path (404) and a missing or wrong token (401) straight after reading the headers, then closed the connection without reading the request body. If the body is still in flight when the socket closes, Windows answers it with a reset, and the reset discards the reply the client has not read yet. A loaded machine delays the body, which is why the failures came under load. The earlier probe (2026-09-23) sent headers and body in one write, so the body was always already buffered and the race never showed; sending the body 150 ms after the headers reproduces it 10 times out of 10. A real client (a stream-deck button, a script with a stale token) could therefore get a connection reset instead of the 404 or 401 that tells it what is wrong. The new `_WebhookHandler._reject` reads the declared body first, up to the accepted maximum (now the named `_MAX_BODY_BYTES`, 64,000) and under the existing 15 s socket timeout, then replies. Both early errors use it. A body over the maximum is still not read, as its docstring says.
- **Result / numbers**: the new `test_e2e_early_error_reply_survives_a_late_body` (404 and 401, three late-body requests each) failed every time on the old code and passes now. Six processes running the e2e tests eight times each, in parallel, lost 6 of 48 runs before the fix and none after. Full suite 11,636 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/desktop_pet/webhook_server.py`, `tests/test_desktop_pet_webhook_server.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-14 · 2026-09-24 · Cap positional parameters at 7 with a guard; keyword-only options are the reasoned exception (closes #24) · #done #tooling #quality

- **What**: `progress.md` #24 filed 31 functions over the workspace's 7-parameter limit. Eleven of them took more than 7 *positional* parameters, where a caller can put a value in the wrong slot without any error, and U-20260924-07 to -13 fixed all eleven by grouping values that belong together: `UpscaleSpec`, `SanitizeSettings`, `ExportSettings`, `ImageQuery`, `DispatcherHooks`, and `(x, y)` / `(w, h)` / RGBA tuples in the GL and minimap helpers. The 17 left all follow one pattern: at most 6 positional parameters, then keyword-only options with defaults. They are the paint engine's `apply_dab`, `flood_fill`, `render_gradient`, `smudge_dab` and similar, two MCP tool handlers whose parameters mirror the tool's JSON schema, and three modules on the #22 unwired list. A keyword-only option cannot land in the wrong slot; wrapping each in its own options object would add a type per function, gain no safety, and churn every engine test. The workspace rules allow a project to list reasoned exceptions, so `CLAUDE.md` now states the project rule: at most 7 positional parameters (`self` / `cls` not counted), anything more keyword-only with a default, and related values grouped as tuples or frozen dataclasses. `tests/test_code_size_limits.py` gains a check for it, because ruff's `PLR0917` is a preview rule and turning on preview also changes `F401` elsewhere. The `pyproject.toml` comment on the ignored `PLR0913` now points to that check. The preview trial did turn up one real dead import, `import urllib.error` in `plugin/plugin_downloader.py`, which is removed.
- **Result / numbers**: functions over 7 positional parameters: 0 (was 11), guarded. 1 new test. Full suite 11,649 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean. #24 is done.
- **Files**: `tests/test_code_size_limits.py`, `CLAUDE.md`, `pyproject.toml`, `Imervue/plugin/plugin_downloader.py`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.

## U-20260924-15 · 2026-09-24 · Log best-effort failures instead of suppressing them; main window first · #refactor #error-handling #system

- **What**: `ruff`'s `BLE` rules (enabled in U-20260923-63) catch `except Exception`, but not `contextlib.suppress(Exception)`, which swallows the same exceptions just as silently. There are 73 of them. The new `system/best_effort.py:best_effort(step, logger=None)` is the logged equivalent. It is a context manager that still swallows any `Exception`, so a failing step never stops the ones after it, but logs a warning naming the step, with the traceback. `KeyboardInterrupt` and `SystemExit` still propagate. The main window's 14 uses move to it: the startup onboarding / What's New check, the plugin shutdown hooks, and every step of the close sequence (folder session, tab signal, watchdog, tree workers, Modify panel, GL textures, geometry, settings, pending deletions). Each is named, so a shutdown bug now shows up in the log. `tests/test_best_effort.py` covers a clean run, a failure being swallowed and logged while later code runs, a custom logger, and base exceptions propagating. `tests/test_main_window_close.py` gains a check that failing close steps are logged by name with their traceback; the existing sequence tests still pass, so which steps a failure skips is unchanged. The other 59 are filed as `progress.md` #25.
- **Result / numbers**: `suppress(Exception)` 73 → 59. 6 new tests. Full suite 11,655 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/system/best_effort.py` (new), `Imervue/Imervue_main_window.py`, `tests/test_best_effort.py`, `tests/test_main_window_close.py`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.

- **What**: while moving the file tree's reveal-in-file-manager code out (U-20260924-16), its macOS branch turned out to run `open "" <path>` whenever `select` was false: the command list was `["open", "-R" if select else "", path]`. `open` rejects the empty argument, so on macOS the context menu's open-containing-folder action (`_open_in_explorer(..., select=False)`) did nothing, and the failure was swallowed until the previous commit. ## U-20260924-25 · 2026-09-24 · Stop the wallpaper action injecting file names into AppleScript · #fix #security #menu

- **What**: "Set as Wallpaper" in the viewer's right-click menu had three faults, all fixed by moving it into the new Qt-free `Imervue/system/wallpaper.py`. (1) On macOS the image path was pasted into the AppleScript source between double quotes, so a file name containing `"` could end the string and run any AppleScript, including `do shell script`. The path now reaches `osascript` as a separate argument, read inside the script as `item 1 of argv`, and is made absolute first so it can never look like an option. (2) On GNOME it passed `file://` plus the raw path, which breaks on spaces, `#` or `%`, and set only `picture-uri`, which GNOME 42+ ignores in the dark style. It now passes a percent-encoded URI from `Path.as_uri()` and sets both `picture-uri` and `picture-uri-dark`. (3) Every failure was swallowed by `suppress(Exception)`, including Windows returning FALSE from `SystemParametersInfoW`. A refused Windows call and a helper that cannot start (`OSError`) are now logged; anything else propagates. `right_click_menu.py` drops its `_set_wallpaper` and its unused `contextlib`, `subprocess` and `sys` imports. New tests in `tests/test_wallpaper.py`: a hostile macOS file name stays out of the script text; the GNOME commands set both keys with a percent-encoded URI; a relative path becomes absolute; every non-Windows command runs; a missing helper is logged and stops; an unexpected error propagates; the Windows call gets the right arguments; a Windows refusal is logged.
- **Result / numbers**: `suppress(Exception)` 2 → 1, none left in `Imervue/`. 9 new tests. Full suite 11,713 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean. `right_click_menu.py` 898 → 870 lines.
- **Files**: `Imervue/system/wallpaper.py` (new), `Imervue/menu/right_click_menu.py`, `tests/test_wallpaper.py`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.
- **Sources**: GNOME 42 dark-style wallpaper key — https://itsfoss.com/dark-light-wallpaper-gnome/
- **Open items**: `progress.md` #25.

## U-20260924-27 · 2026-09-24 · Treat a corrupt WebP EXIF block as unreadable instead of crashing · #fix #image #exif

- **What**: a WebP whose EXIF chunk lacks a valid TIFF header makes Pillow's `getexif()` raise `SyntaxError` ("not a TIFF file"), which `IMAGE_READ_ERRORS` did not cover. Every EXIF reader narrowed to that tuple therefore crashed on such a file instead of falling back: the organizer's and sanitizer's date lookup, the token-rename metadata, and the metadata export. Three other readers listed their own `(OSError, ValueError)` and had the same hole: GPS extraction, the EXIF orientation read used when viewing and rotating, and the calendar index's capture date. `SyntaxError` joins `IMAGE_READ_ERRORS`, and those three readers now use the tuple. The orientation read also catches `TypeError` for a non-numeric orientation tag. Found by fuzzing: flipping random bytes inside the EXIF block of JPEG, PNG, WebP and TIFF files 4,000 times each raised `SyntaxError` 432 times, all from WebP, and nothing else escaped. The regular fuzz test in `tests/test_read_errors.py` now calls `getexif()` too. New tests: a deterministic corrupt-EXIF WebP (`corrupt_exif_webp()`) raises inside `IMAGE_READ_ERRORS`, and the organizer date, GPS extraction, orientation read and calendar date all fall back on it. The new tests failed against the previous commit in a separate worktree.
- **Result / numbers**: 5 new tests, 1 strengthened. Full suite 11,720 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/image/read_errors.py`, `Imervue/image/gps.py`, `Imervue/image/orientation.py`, `Imervue/library/calendar_index.py`, `tests/test_read_errors.py`, `tests/test_image_organizer.py`, `tests/test_gps.py`, `tests/test_orientation.py`, `tests/test_calendar_index.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-30 · 2026-09-24 · Narrow the gpu_image_view/ silent broad handlers (#26) · #refactor #error-handling #gpu_image_view

- **What**: `progress.md` #26 continued with its four `gpu_image_view/` handlers. `paintGL` abandons a frame only on `GLError` from `glClear` (context torn down) and still closes native painting. The memory-pressure indicator skips a refresh only on `RuntimeError` from its source, which is what a viewer deleted during shutdown raises. The drag-out preview returns no pixmap only for `IMAGE_READ_ERRORS`. The vendor VRAM probe reads 0 ("unsupported") only for `GLError` or `KeyError`. Those two were measured on a real context: an offscreen `QOpenGLContext` on this machine answered both the NVIDIA and AMD enums, raised `GLError` with no context current, and raised `KeyError` ("Unknown specifier") for an enum PyOpenGL has no size entry for. Any other error now propagates in all four. New tests: `tests/test_vram_detect.py` covers scalar and vector readings, both "unsupported" errors, an unexpected error propagating, and the NVIDIA → AMD fallback. `tests/test_gpu_image_view_paint_guard.py` drives `paintGL` unbound with a fake painter: a dead context abandons the frame with native painting closed, and an unexpected error propagates. The drag preview and the memory indicator each gain an unexpected-error test.
- **Result / numbers**: 20 → 16 silent handlers under #26, none left in `gpu_image_view/`. 13 new tests. Full suite 11,754 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.

## U-20260924-32 · 2026-09-24 · Stop an old Tesseract from quitting the app through the OCR probe · #fix #ocr #error-handling

- **What**: `ocr_available()` guarded its Tesseract probe with `except Exception`, but `pytesseract.get_tesseract_version()` raises `SystemExit` ("Invalid tesseract version") when the installed Tesseract is older than pytesseract supports. `SystemExit` is not an `Exception`, so with an old Tesseract on the PATH, opening the OCR dialog, or an MCP OCR request, would have quit the whole application. The probe now catches exactly what pytesseract raises: `ImportError` (package absent), `OSError` (binary missing; `TesseractNotFoundError` is an `OSError`), `subprocess.CalledProcessError` (`tesseract --version` exits non-zero), and `SystemExit` for the version check, which is also logged as a warning. Any other error propagates. The failures are still not cached, so installing Tesseract later still self-heals. `extract_words` also opened a path argument with `Image.open` and never closed it; it now closes the image after the OCR call. This removes the `image/ocr.py` entry from `progress.md` #26. New tests: an old Tesseract reports unavailable and logs instead of exiting; a failing version probe reports unavailable; an unexpected probe error propagates; the image opened from a path is closed after OCR. Three of the four failed against the previous commit in a separate worktree. The probe-failure test also passed there, since `except Exception` already caught `CalledProcessError`.
- **Result / numbers**: 4 new tests. Full suite 11,774 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/image/ocr.py`, `tests/test_ocr.py`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.
- **Open items**: `progress.md` #26.

## U-20260924-36 · 2026-09-24 · Share one censor implementation between the app and the frozen-build runner · #done #fix #refactor #safety_review #plugins

- **What**: `progress.md` #28 finished. `plugins/safety_review/_runner.py` is the script the frozen build runs in an external Python, because the frozen app cannot import torch; that Python cannot import the Qt plugin package. The runner therefore carried its own copies of the censor helpers and all the shared constants, and the copies had drifted:
  - Its ellipse mask filled the whole box instead of the inset ellipse (`ELLIPSE_COVER_FRAC` 0.85) the in-app path uses. A 40×40 box got 1,248 censored pixels instead of 1,012.
  - It left a 1×1 detection **uncensored** (0 of 1 pixel): Pillow does not draw a degenerate ellipse, and the in-app path falls back to a solid fill for that case.
  - Its colour-count heuristic still looped over `Image.getdata()`, which Pillow 14 removes.
- **How**: A comparison test run before the change showed everything else identical: 34 of 45 cases, with only the ellipse / precise shapes and the 1-px box differing. The pure helpers moved verbatim from `_detection.py` into the new flat module `_censor_core.py`: box expansion and shrinking, junction bridges and merge gap, censored region, shape masks, region censoring, image-mode detection, and parent-directory creation. `_detection.py` imports them. The runner and `finetune.py` import `_censor_core` and `_constants` by `__package__`: as package modules in-app and in tests, as sibling files when run as scripts. The runner's copied constants, category maps and helpers are gone, and the pinned model SHA now lives only in `_constants.py`. `_detect_image_mode` also closes the image it opens.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_safety_review_runner_parity.py` pins that the runner and `_detection` use the very same helper objects. It also checks that the runner censors a 1-px box for ellipse and precise shapes and uses the inset ellipse. Its script tests copy `_runner.py` (and `finetune.py`) alone with their siblings into a temporary directory and run them there with the plugin package off `sys.path`. Existing tests now reach the moved helpers through `_censor_core`.
- **Result / numbers**: `_detection.py` + `_runner.py` + `finetune.py` lost 414 lines and gained 60; `_censor_core.py` is 242. `safety_review` is now 15 files / 4,554 lines. The plugin table in `architecture_explore.md` was recounted: every plugin row had drifted slightly. 9 new tests. Full suite 11,811 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean. Mirrored to `Imervue_Plugins` on `main`.
- **Files**: `plugins/safety_review/_censor_core.py` (new), `_detection.py`, `_runner.py`, `finetune.py`, `tests/test_safety_review_runner_parity.py` (new), `tests/test_safety_review_core.py`, `architecture_explore.md`, `progress.md`.
- **Open items**: `progress.md` #29.

## U-20260924-37 · 2026-09-24 · Plugins import shared helpers instead of copying them; faster object labelling · #refactor #perf #plugins #Imervue_Plugins

- **What**: a scan for identical function bodies across `plugins/` found helpers copied from plugin to plugin, where the main program already had the same code.
  - **Copies replaced by imports.** Ten image plugins each carried `_load_rgba`: `ai_colorize`, `ai_denoise`, `ai_motion_deblur`, `ai_object_remove`, `ai_outpaint`, `ai_portrait_relight`, `ai_smart_resize`, `ai_style_transfer`, `npr_filters`, `portrait_mode`. They now import `Imervue.gui._apply_save.load_rgba` under the same local name, so call sites and existing test patches are unchanged. `ai_background_remover`, `object_splitter` and `safety_review` each copied `_subprocess_kwargs`; they import it from `Imervue.plugin.pip_installer`, where they already get `_find_python`. Git history shows `load_rgba` shipped in v1.0.56 and the `_subprocess_kwargs` export in 1.0.21, so newly downloaded plugins still run on older installs. `load_rgba` itself now closes the file with `with`.
  - **object_splitter.** The plugin and its standalone `_runner.py` each had a pure-Python BFS `_connected_components`. It moves to the flat sibling `_components.py`, loaded the same way as `safety_review`'s `_censor_core.py`. Measured on a 2000×2000 mask, the BFS takes 2.17 s, about 13 s for a 24-megapixel photo. `scipy.ndimage.label` takes 0.013 s and gave identical labels, same numbering and dtype, on 300 random masks. rembg 2.0.85, which the plugin requires, declares `scipy>=1.16.3` in its wheel metadata. `_components` now uses SciPy and falls back to the BFS without it. NumPy and SciPy are imported inside the functions, because the runner adds the environment's site-packages to `sys.path` only after loading its siblings.
  - **Contracts.** Documented in `architecture.md` §6 here and §5 of `Imervue_Plugins/architecture.md`, which also states the rule for sibling modules.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_object_splitter_components.py`: SciPy labels equal the BFS on random masks; 4-connectivity and raster numbering; the fallback without SciPy; the plugin and runner share the helper; the runner runs as a script with only its sibling; neither module needs NumPy at load time. `tests/test_safety_review_runner_parity.py` gains the same load-time check for `_constants`, `_censor_core` and `_runner`. `tests/test_apply_save.py` checks that `load_rgba` converts, keeps RGBA as is, and closes the file.
- **Result / numbers**: 13 plugin copies removed, 101 lines. The plugin table in `architecture_explore.md` recounted. 11 new tests. Full suite 11,823 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean. Mirrored to `Imervue_Plugins` on `main`.
