## U-20260924-39 · 2026-09-24 · Stop unpickling the semantic-search cache · #fix #security #library

- **What**: `ClipSearchIndex.load` read its semantic-search cache with `np.load(..., allow_pickle=True)`. The paths were saved as a NumPy object array, which is pickled, so loading unpickled whatever the `.npz` held. Anyone able to replace that file, in the app-data cache directory or wherever `load(path)` points, could have code run on the next semantic search. The paint document loader already enforced `allow_pickle=False`; this was the last `allow_pickle=True` in the tree. The paths are now stored as UTF-8 JSON bytes in a `uint8` array (`paths_json`). `load` opens the archive with `allow_pickle=False` inside `with`, which also closes the file it used to leave open. It requires the decoded value to be a list of strings, and catches `zipfile.BadZipFile` besides the old errors. A cache written in the old format is rejected like a corrupt one (logged) and the index is rebuilt; nothing in it is unpickled. The now-unused `pickle` import is gone.
- **Tests**: a saved cache holds no object arrays and round-trips a non-ASCII path. A legacy cache whose path array holds an object that records being unpickled is rejected, and the object never runs. A JSON value that is not a list is rejected. Run against the previous commit in a separate worktree, the legacy-cache test failed: the old loader did unpickle the payload.
- **Result / numbers**: `allow_pickle=True` uses 1 → 0. 3 new tests. Full suite 11,828 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/library/clip_search.py`, `tests/test_clip_search.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-40 · 2026-09-24 · Guard against unpickling in NumPy and PyTorch loads · #security #tests

- **What**: a guard so the semantic-search cache problem (U-20260924-39) cannot come back unnoticed. Bandit flags `pickle.load` and unsafe `yaml.load`, but not the NumPy and PyTorch forms that unpickle just the same. The new `tests/test_no_unsafe_deserialization.py` walks `Imervue/` and `plugins/` with `ast`. It rejects `np.load` whose `allow_pickle` is anything but a literal `False`, and `torch.load` without a literal `weights_only=True`. A self-test pins the flagged and allowed forms, and the detector flags line 304 of the pre-fix `clip_search.py`. `CLAUDE.md` lists the rule under "Rules the tools do not catch".
- **Result / numbers**: no current code is flagged. 2 new tests. Full suite 11,830 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `tests/test_no_unsafe_deserialization.py` (new), `CLAUDE.md`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-41 · 2026-09-24 · Refuse plugin and file names that could leave the plugins folder · #fix #security #plugin

- **What**: the plugin downloader turned names from the distribution repository's git tree straight into local paths. `parse_plugin_tree` splits a tree path on `/` only, so a plugin or file name pushed from Linux could still carry a backslash, a drive colon or a bare `..`. `DownloadPluginWorker` then wrote `plugins_dir() / plugin / file` and, before swapping in the new copy, ran `shutil.rmtree(plugins_dir() / plugin)`. A plugin named `..` would therefore have deleted the directory above the plugins folder, and a file named `..\..\x.py` would have been written outside it on Windows. The repository is ours, but the downloader is the last line of defence if it is ever compromised. The new `is_safe_path_component` accepts only a name that is one plain file or directory name on every platform. It rejects empty names, `.`, `..`, separators, Windows-forbidden characters, control characters and a trailing dot or space. `parse_plugin_tree` skips plugins and files whose names fail it. `DownloadPluginWorker.run` checks every name again and reports an error before it creates, downloads or deletes anything. `architecture.md` §6 states the rule.
- **Tests**: 22 name cases, safe and unsafe; a listing whose unsafe plugin and file are skipped while the safe ones stay; a download named `..` refused with a sibling file left untouched and no request made; a file name with a backslash traversal refused. The listing and worker tests failed against the previous commit in a separate worktree.
- **Result / numbers**: 25 new tests. Full suite 11,855 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/plugin/plugin_downloader.py`, `tests/test_plugin_downloader.py`, `tests/test_plugin_downloader_partial.py`, `architecture.md`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-42 · 2026-09-24 · Refuse puppet archives that expand past 2 GiB · #fix #security #puppet

- **What**: loading a `.puppet` rig read every archive entry into memory with no size check, so a small crafted archive (a zip bomb, a few kilobytes on disk) could exhaust memory the moment it was opened. `_load_from_zip`, used by both `load_puppet` and `from_zip_bytes`, now sums the declared uncompressed sizes first and raises `PuppetFormatError` above 2 GiB. `zipfile` never reads past an entry's declared size, so checking the declared total before reading covers it. Rig textures are PNGs that barely compress (the bundled example is about 21 MB), so the ceiling is far above any real rig. Paint project files get no such check: their transparent layers legitimately compress at bomb-like ratios.
- **Tests**: an archive under 64 KB that expands past a lowered limit is refused; one under the limit still loads; the check runs before any entry is opened.
- **Result / numbers**: 3 new tests. Full suite 11,858 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/puppet/document_io.py`, `tests/test_puppet_document_io.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-43 · 2026-09-24 · Keep web pages out of the puppet's VTube Studio API · #fix #security #puppet

- **What**: the puppet's opt-in VTube Studio API server listens on `ws://127.0.0.1:8001` and issues an authentication token to any client that asks. That keeps the LAN out, but not the browser: any web page the user has open may open a WebSocket to localhost (cross-site WebSocket hijacking). Such a page could authenticate itself, then inject and create parameters to drive the puppet. Browsers always send an `Origin` header; native trackers do not. The new `is_allowed_origin` admits:
  - no origin, `null` or `file://` (native apps, pages opened from disk);
  - localhost origins (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`), so a tracker served locally keeps working;
  - non-web schemes such as browser extensions.
  It refuses any other `http(s)` host, including look-alikes such as `localhost.evil.example`. `_on_new_connection` closes a refused socket with a warning naming the origin, before creating a session. Accepted sockets are capped at 1 MiB per message and per frame; parameter messages are a few dozen floats. This PySide6 exposes the caps on `QWebSocket`, not the server. The module docstring states the threat and the rule.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_puppet_vts_origin.py` runs on CI because it builds no GL widget. It covers 9 allowed and 5 refused origins, and drives `_on_new_connection` unbound with fake sockets: a native tracker gets a session with both caps set; a web page is closed and logged, while a localhost tracker in the same batch is accepted.
- **Result / numbers**: 16 new tests. Full suite 11,874 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/puppet/vts_api.py`, `tests/test_puppet_vts_origin.py` (new), `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-44 · 2026-09-24 · Refuse web-page requests to the desktop pet's webhook · #fix #security #desktop_pet

- **What**: the desktop pet's webhook receiver (`POST /trigger` on 127.0.0.1) authenticates with an optional bearer token whose default is empty. A browser lets any site send a cross-origin `POST` to localhost, and a `text/plain` body triggers no preflight. With the default settings, any web page the user had open could therefore make the pet play a motion or display a speech bubble with the page's text. Browsers always send `Origin` on such requests; curl, Stream Deck and other native clients do not. The receiver now answers 403 `forbidden origin` to a request whose `Origin` is a remote web site, before the auth check and before anything is dispatched.
- **Shared helper**: `is_allowed_origin`, introduced for the puppet's VTube Studio API in U-20260924-43, moves to the new `Imervue/system/local_origin.py`, which both servers import. No origin, `null`, `file://`, localhost and non-web schemes pass; any other `http(s)` host is refused. Both module docstrings state the threat.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_local_origin.py` holds the 15 origin cases moved from the VTS test. The webhook gains two end-to-end tests against a real server: a remote origin gets 403 and nothing is dispatched; a localhost origin is accepted and dispatched.
- **Result / numbers**: 2 new tests (the origin cases moved). Full suite 11,877 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/system/local_origin.py` (new), `Imervue/desktop_pet/webhook_server.py`, `Imervue/puppet/vts_api.py`, `tests/test_local_origin.py` (new), `tests/test_puppet_vts_origin.py`, `tests/test_desktop_pet_webhook_server.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-45 · 2026-09-24 · Require Pillow 12.3.0 or later · #fix #security #deps

- **What**: the runtime dependency list named `Pillow` with no version, while the frozen build and `uv.lock` both use 12.3.0. A user running `pip install Imervue` into an environment that already had an older Pillow kept it: pip does not upgrade a satisfied requirement. Releases before 12.3.0 carry fixes that matter to an image viewer, which opens files from anywhere: an out-of-bounds write on a crafted PSD (CVE-2026-25990, fixed in 12.1.1), a FITS GZIP decompression bomb (CVE-2026-40192, fixed in 12.1.1), and heap corruption in `ImageCmsTransform.apply` (CVE-2026-59205, fixed in 12.3.0). The code also relies on newer APIs such as `Image.Resampling`. `Pillow>=12.3.0` is now the floor in `pyproject.toml`, `dev.toml`, `requirements.txt` and `dev_requirements.txt`, which the packaging tests keep in step.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_release_workflow_pins.py` gains a check that every version pinned in `release.yml` satisfies the specifier `requirements.txt` declares for it, so the frozen build can never ship below the floor.
- **Result / numbers**: 1 new test. Full suite 11,878 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Sources**: https://www.sentinelone.com/vulnerability-database/cve-2026-59205/ · https://security.snyk.io/package/pip/pillow
- **Files**: `pyproject.toml`, `dev.toml`, `requirements.txt`, `dev_requirements.txt`, `tests/test_release_workflow_pins.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-46 · 2026-09-24 · Move CI to Node 24 actions pinned by commit · #ci #security #deps

- **What**: every workflow step used a Node 20 action: `actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/setup-python@v5`, and `actions/upload-artifact@v4` / `actions/download-artifact@v4`. GitHub made Node 24 the runner default on 2026-06-16 and removed Node 20 on 2026-09-23. The references were also mutable tags, and the 2025 tj-actions/changed-files compromise showed that a tag can be repointed at malicious code. All 17 references in `test.yml` and `release.yml` now point at the latest releases, whose `action.yml` declares `node24`, each pinned to its commit SHA with the version as a comment: checkout v7.0.1, setup-python v7.0.0, upload-artifact v7.0.1, download-artifact v8.0.1. None of the breaking changes from the release notes affects these workflows:
  - download-artifact v5 changed paths only for downloads by ID; these download by name.
  - download-artifact v8 now fails on a digest mismatch, which is wanted.
  - setup-python v7 removed the `pip-install` input, which is unused.
  - checkout v6 stores credentials in a separate included file, which the release job's `git push` still uses.
  - checkout v7 blocks fork checkouts under `pull_request_target` / `workflow_run`, which are not used.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_workflow_actions.py` requires, in every workflow, each `uses:` to name a 40-hex commit followed by a `# vX.Y.Z` comment, and each action to appear at a single commit across workflows. The previous `test.yml` fails it.
- **Result / numbers**: 4 new tests. Full suite 11,882 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean. The workflows take effect on the next push.
- **Sources**: https://github.blog/changelog/2025-09-19-deprecation-of-node-20-on-github-actions-runners/ and each action's release notes (`gh api repos/actions/<name>/releases`).
- **Files**: `.github/workflows/test.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `tests/test_workflow_actions.py` (new), `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-47 · 2026-09-24 · Send Dependabot updates to dev and keep the action pins current · #ci #deps

- **What**: `.github/dependabot.yml` covered only pip and opened its pull requests against `main`, where every push runs the release workflow. The workspace's other repositories already target `dev`; `D:\Codes\progress.md` X-16 listed Imervue as one of the two left behind. The pip updates now target `dev`. A `github-actions` entry, also on `dev` and weekly, keeps the commit-pinned actions from U-20260924-46 current. Dependabot updates a SHA pin together with its version comment.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_workflow_actions.py` checks that `dependabot.yml` covers pip and GitHub Actions and that every entry targets `dev`. It reads the file as text, because PyYAML is not a test dependency.
- **Result / numbers**: 1 new test. Full suite 11,883 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `.github/dependabot.yml`, `tests/test_workflow_actions.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-48 · 2026-09-24 · Make the Cubism signature test pass on Python 3.10 · #tests #ci #puppet

- **What**: `dev` is 138 commits ahead of `origin`, so CI's Python 3.10–3.13 matrix had not yet seen any of this round's changes. The fast layer was run locally exactly as `test.yml` runs it: `--test-layer=fast`, `-p no:unraisableexception`, the workflow's pip package list, in `uv` virtual environments for CPython 3.10.20, 3.11 and 3.13. One test failed, on 3.10 only: `test_bind_signatures_declares_every_function_exactly` compared the `repr` of each Cubism Core ctypes signature. Python 3.10 shows `POINTER(c_float)` as `ctypes.wintypes.LP_c_float` once `ctypes.wintypes` is imported; later versions show `ctypes.LP_c_float`. The test now names each type structurally through `_describe`: pointers as `*`, arrays as `type[n]`, others by `__name__`. The expected table was regenerated with it and gives byte-identical output on 3.10 and 3.14. The binding code is unchanged.
- **Result / numbers**: fast layer passes on 3.10 (8,539 passed, 45 skipped, the extra skips being the existing `tomllib` guards), 3.11 and 3.13 (8,544 passed, 40 skipped each). Full suite on 3.14: 11,883 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `tests/test_puppet_cubism_native_bridge.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-49 · 2026-09-24 · Pin the upscale models to commits and verify the face-landmark model · #fix #security #supply-chain

- **What**: two model downloads could hand the app whatever the server returned.
  - **AI upscale.** `_download_model` called `hf_hub_download(..., revision=info.get("revision", "main"))`, but none of the three `UPSCALE_MODELS` entries carried a `revision`, so every download followed `main`. That broke the `CLAUDE.md` rule (a pinned revision, bandit B615), while the docstring claimed the opposite. Each entry now pins the commit its `main` points at today, looked up through the Hugging Face API: `OwlMaster/AllFilesRope` `d783e61…` for both general models, `xiongjie/lightweight-real-ESRGAN-anime` `695895c…` for the anime model. The file lists confirm the files are present. The weights users receive are unchanged, and Hugging Face caches by commit, so existing caches still serve them offline. `_download_model` now reads `info["revision"]` with no fallback.
  - **Puppet webcam.** `face_landmarker.task` came from Google's bucket unverified, and any non-empty cached file was trusted. The file (3,758,596 bytes, unchanged since 2023-05-03) was downloaded once; its MD5 matched the bucket's ETag, and its SHA-256 is now pinned. A cached copy is used only if it matches, and a mismatch is logged and re-downloaded. A download that does not match is refused and nothing is written. The verified file is written through a `.part` file and `os.replace`, so a failed write cannot leave a truncated model that later looks cached.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_ai_upscale.py` checks that every model pins a 40-hex commit and that the download passes it. The new `tests/test_puppet_webcam_model.py` runs on CI because it builds no GL widget, unlike the webcam test file it takes the two model-path tests from. It covers a verified cache reused offline, a first-use download saved without a `.part` left over, a tampered cache replaced, a wrong-hash download refused and not saved, and a network failure reported.
- **Result / numbers**: 8 new tests (2 moved). Full suite 11,889 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/gui/ai_upscale_dialog.py`, `Imervue/puppet/webcam_tracker.py`, `tests/test_ai_upscale.py`, `tests/test_puppet_webcam_model.py` (new), `tests/test_puppet_webcam_tracker.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-50 · 2026-09-24 · Verify the embeddable Python and install pip from a pinned wheel · #fix #security #supply-chain #plugin

- **What**: when a plugin needs pip and no suitable interpreter exists, `_DownloadPythonWorker` sets one up.
  - **Before.** It downloaded CPython 3.12.8's embeddable zip and extracted it unverified. It then downloaded `https://bootstrap.pypa.io/get-pip.py` and **ran it**. That URL serves whatever the current script is, so its content changes with every pip release and cannot be pinned. TLS was the only protection.
  - **The zip.** It is now checked against a pinned SHA-256 before extraction. The digest was computed from a download that matches python.org's published MD5 (`1e86b04b…`) and size (11,094,114 bytes).
  - **pip.** `get-pip.py` is gone. pip 26.2.1 comes as a wheel from files.pythonhosted.org, checked against PyPI's published SHA-256. It is installed by running pip from the wheel itself through `runpy.run_module('pip', alter_sys=True)`. The two obvious forms fail: `python pip.whl/pip install` makes pip refuse to modify itself on Windows, and `PYTHONPATH` is ignored because of the embeddable Python's `._pth` file. Both failures were seen, and the `runpy` form was proven, on a real embeddable 3.12.8 before the code changed.
  - **Shared check.** `_download_verified` reports a failed download or a checksum mismatch through `result_ready`, and nothing is extracted, written or run. The wheel is deleted after installation.
- **End-to-end check**: the real worker was run against the network into a scratch directory. It downloaded and verified both files, extracted the zip, patched `._pth`, installed pip 26.2.1 from the wheel, passed `_verify_python`, and left no wheel behind.
- **Tests**: the new `tests/test_pip_installer_embed.py` fakes the network and child process. It covers: pinned digests are SHA-256 values; the happy path runs pip from the wheel and cleans up; a tampered zip is refused before extraction; a tampered wheel is never run or kept; a failed pip install is reported.
- **Result / numbers**: 6 new tests. Full suite 11,895 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Sources**: https://www.python.org/api/v2/downloads/release_file/?release=935 (MD5, size), https://pypi.org/pypi/pip/json (wheel digest).
- **Files**: `Imervue/plugin/pip_installer.py`, `tests/test_pip_installer_embed.py` (new), `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-51 · 2026-09-24 · Move the plugin interpreter to CPython 3.12.10 · #deps #security #plugin

- **What**: the embeddable Python the plugin installer sets up moves from CPython 3.12.8 (2024-12) to 3.12.10 (2025-04-08). 3.12 is in security-only maintenance, and python.org publishes no embeddable zip after 3.12.10. So 3.12.10 is the newest build that can be downloaded, and it brings two patch releases of fixes. Moving to 3.13 is not possible: the frozen build runs Python 3.12 (`release.yml`) and imports the packages this interpreter installs, so their compiled `cp312` extensions must match its minor version. The pinned SHA-256 was recomputed from a download that matches python.org's published MD5 (`fe8ef205…`) and size (11,133,606 bytes). The real worker was run end to end again into a scratch directory: it downloaded and verified 3.12.10, installed pip 26.2.1 from the pinned wheel, and passed verification. The source comment now states the minor-version constraint.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_pip_installer_embed.py` checks that the embeddable Python's minor version equals the one `release.yml` builds with.
- **Result / numbers**: 1 new test. Full suite 11,896 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean. Existing installs keep their interpreter; the installer only downloads when none is present.
- **Files**: `Imervue/plugin/pip_installer.py`, `tests/test_pip_installer_embed.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-52 · 2026-09-24 · Keep the desktop pet's credentials out of the support bundle · #fix #security #privacy

- **What**: Help's support bundle (`system/error_report.build_report`) zips the log, system info and a "sanitised" copy of the user settings, meant to be sent to a maintainer. Sanitising only removed a fixed list of **top-level** personal-data keys. The desktop pet keeps its secrets inside the nested `desktop_pet` settings dict: the webhook bearer token, the OBS WebSocket password and the Twitch OAuth token. Once set, all three went into the bundle verbatim. `sanitise_settings` now also walks every dict and list at any depth. A key whose name marks a credential keeps its name, so a maintainer can still see that it was set, but a non-empty value becomes `<redacted>`. The new `is_credential_key` decides from the `_`-separated parts of the name: a `password` / `passwd` / `secret` / `oauth` / `credential(s)` part, or a name ending in `token`, `api_key`, `apikey` or `client_id`. Settings like `token_rename_template` or `tokens_used` are kept. The input dict is not modified. A scan of every logging call found none passing a credential-named value, so the bundled log was already clean.
- **Tests**: 14 name cases, credential and not; nested and in-list redaction with key names kept and the input untouched; and an end-to-end bundle test proving neither fake pet credential appears anywhere in the zip. Run against the previous commit in a separate worktree, the bundle test failed: the old code shipped the tokens.
- **Result / numbers**: 3 new tests. Full suite 11,899 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/system/error_report.py`, `tests/test_error_report.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-53 · 2026-09-24 · Show EXIF values and file names as text in the sidebar · #fix #security #gui

- **What**: the EXIF sidebar is a rich-text `QLabel`. It interpolated values taken from the image file straight into its HTML: every EXIF field (make, model, lens and the rest, plus the pixel dimensions), the file name, a video's codec, and the reverse-geocoded place name inside the map link. A crafted file could therefore inject markup. It could fake or bury fields with its own tags, or make Qt load a local image with `<img src=...>`. Ordinary values containing `&` or `<` also rendered wrongly. Links were already inert: external links are off and only the map link is handled. Each of those values now goes through `html.escape` before interpolation; labels from the language table and numbers formatted by the code are left as they are. An AST scan over `Imervue/` and `plugins/` for HTML f-strings with unescaped values found nothing else: the search dialog already escapes, and the other hits are constants or numbers.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_exif_sidebar.py` checks that a hostile value (`<img …><b>…</b> & Co`) comes out escaped in the EXIF lines, the dimensions, the video codec and the map link (which stays the only `<a>`), and that a file name containing `<` and `&` is escaped by `update_info`. All four failed against the previous commit in a separate worktree.
- **Result / numbers**: 4 new tests. Full suite 11,903 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/gui/exif_sidebar.py`, `tests/test_exif_sidebar.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-54 · 2026-09-24 · Pin Leaflet with Subresource Integrity; fit single places at city zoom · #fix #security #gui

- **What**: the map view renders Leaflet 1.9.4 in a `QWebEngineView`, loading `leaflet.js` and `leaflet.css` from unpkg with no integrity check. A tampered CDN copy would run inside the app's web engine. Both tags now carry Leaflet's published Subresource Integrity hashes with `crossorigin=""`. The hashes were computed from the unpkg files and match the values on leafletjs.com's download page.
  - **Checked in the real engine.** The page was rendered in QtWebEngine 6.11.2 and screenshotted: with the correct hashes the map loads; with one hash altered, the page stays blank (a 1,967-byte capture), so the check is enforced.
  - **Zoom.** The same render showed a second problem: with a single place, `fitBounds` zoomed to street level (house numbers, nothing recognisable around them). It is now capped at zoom 12, city level.
  - **Tiles.** OpenStreetMap blocks browser tile requests that break its usage policy. They were checked with and without a `Referer`, in the real engine and with browser-like headers: this page currently gets real tiles, so nothing changes there. A client with no identifying headers does get the "Access blocked" tile.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_map_view.py` checks that each Leaflet tag carries its published hash and that the fit zoom cap is filled in.
- **Result / numbers**: 2 new tests. Full suite 11,905 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Sources**: https://leafletjs.com/download.html (SRI hashes), https://operations.osmfoundation.org/policies/tiles/
- **Files**: `Imervue/gui/map_view_dialog.py`, `tests/test_map_view.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-55 · 2026-09-24 · Load rawpy and imageio only when a RAW file is decoded · #perf #startup

- **What**: `-X importtime` showed that `gpu_image_view/images/image_loader.py` and `load_thumbnail_worker.py` imported `rawpy` and `imageio` at module level. Both are needed only to decode a RAW file (the embedded JPEG preview goes through imageio), yet every start paid for them before a window appeared. The imports now sit in the two RAW decode functions. An interleaved benchmark of 10 fresh interpreters importing the main window, previous commit against this change: median 563 → 496 ms, fastest run 479 → 386 ms, with neither module loaded any more.
- **Test fixes the change exposed**:
  - `tests/test_image_info.py` installed an empty `rawpy` module into `sys.modules` at collection time whenever rawpy had not been imported yet, and never removed it. It had never taken effect, because `image_loader` always imported the real rawpy first. With the import deferred, the stub leaked into every later test module; running the fast layer serially, as CI does, broke collection of `test_load_thumbnail_worker.py`. rawpy is a required dependency, so the stub is removed.
  - `tests/test_load_thumbnail_worker.py` patched `rawpy.imread` through the worker module's `rawpy` attribute. It now patches the `rawpy` module itself, which the lazy import sees.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_image_loader.py` imports the main window in a fresh interpreter and checks that neither `rawpy` nor `imageio` gets loaded. The fast layer passes serially, CI-style, on Python 3.13: 8,562 passed, 39 skipped.
- **Result / numbers**: 1 new test. Full suite 11,906 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/gpu_image_view/images/image_loader.py`, `load_thumbnail_worker.py`, `tests/test_image_loader.py`, `tests/test_image_info.py`, `tests/test_load_thumbnail_worker.py`, `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-56 · 2026-09-24 · Translate 334 UI strings that every language showed in English · #i18n #gui

- **What**: the UI looks strings up as `lang.get("key", "English text")`, and a missing key quietly falls back to the English text. An AST scan of every lookup on the language dictionary (`lang`, `self._lang`, `language_wrapper.language_word_dict`) found **334 keys that no dictionary defined**, used at 350 call sites in 72 modules. Every Chinese, Japanese and Korean user saw English there with no error. The biggest gaps were the paint workspace (brushes dock 27, manga menu 19, status bar 14, preset dialog 12, colour dock 12, tabs 11, tool bar 9), the folder tree (11), bookmarks, hierarchical tags, the plugin installer and several filter dialogs. The keys and their English text were extracted from the code; the two defaults held in constants were resolved by hand. Each key was then translated into Traditional Chinese (Taiwan usage), Simplified Chinese, Japanese and Korean, following each dictionary's existing terms, keeping every `{placeholder}` and line break, and checked by script. Two terms were aligned with the dictionaries: 資源回收桶 in zh-TW, and the Korean label 표준 for the Normal blend mode. The zh-TW text was scanned for Mainland terms and none were found. All five dictionaries gained the 334 keys, sorted, at their ends.
- **Guard**: the new `tests/test_translation_keys_defined.py` fails when code looks up a literal key that `english.py` lacks; a self-test pins which lookups it counts. The existing `test_translations.py` and `test_language.py` already keep the other languages in step with English, placeholders included.
- **Result / numbers**: 334 × 5 entries added; 2 new tests. Full suite 11,908 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/multi_language/english.py`, `traditional_chinese.py`, `chinese.py`, `japanese.py`, `korean.py`, `tests/test_translation_keys_defined.py` (new), `architecture_explore.md`.

- **What**: the same survey as U-20260924-56, run over the bundled plugins. It compared the keys each plugin looks up with the keys its own `get_translations()` defines, since a plugin can be installed alone. ## U-20260924-59 · 2026-09-24 · Translate runtime-built UI keys and the built-in material names · #i18n #paint

- **What**: a follow-up to U-20260924-56. That guard sees only literal keys, while about 25 UI call sites build the key at runtime (`lang.get(f"paint_tool_{tool}", …)`). A survey expanded those whose value list is a constant. It found 62 missing keys, and a real-app screenshot in Traditional Chinese found a further gap. The missing keys, all shown in English in every language:
  - the Quick Select tool label;
  - the sumi brush and the Liquify "push left" mode;
  - the Timeline View submenu (by day / month / year);
  - the undo / redo status messages (including "Nothing to undo");
  - the image-quality metric names;
  - the pick / reject / unflag culling toasts;
  - all 12 adjustment-layer kinds;
  - the 6 comic stamps and their tooltips;
  - the 10 page templates of the New Project dialog and the add-page menu, whose fallback was the raw id (`manga_b5_4koma`), so even English showed the id. They now read "Manga B5, 4-koma", "Webtoon strip (800 × 3200)" and so on.
  - The screenshot's gap was the materials dock: it printed the nine built-in procedural materials' English names ("Dot 30%", "Coarse paper") verbatim. The new `material_display_name` looks them up under a separate `paint_material_builtin_` prefix and falls back to the entry's own name, so a user's material file keeps its name, even one called "All".
- **Translations**: five of the 62 reuse existing entries with identical English, such as Levels, Gradient Map and Channel Mixer. The rest were translated into all four languages following each dictionary's terms, and the nine material names by hand following the dictionaries' 網點 / 紋理 wording. All 71 keys were checked by script for placeholders.
- **Guard**: `tests/test_translation_dynamic_keys.py` expands each family from its source constant (`TOOL_ORDER`, the page templates, `ADJUSTMENT_KINDS`, blend modes, brush kinds, `WARP_KINDS`, material categories, the culling fallbacks, the quality metrics, the comic stamp library, timeline and history kinds). It requires every resulting key in `english.py`, and checks that the built-in materials translate while user materials do not.
- **Result / numbers**: 71 × 5 entries added; 15 new tests. Full suite 11,942 passed, 1 skipped; ruff and bandit clean.
- **Files**: `Imervue/paint/docks/materials.py`, the five `Imervue/multi_language/` dictionaries, `tests/test_translation_dynamic_keys.py` (new), `architecture_explore.md`.

## U-20260924-61 · 2026-09-24 · Paint tab keys have one owner each; 22 dead keys work again · #fix #paint #shortcuts

Twenty-two keys did nothing on the Paint tab. Each one had two enabled shortcuts on it in the same window. Qt treats that as ambiguous and fires neither, silently. Pressing the keys in the live window (canvas focused) showed:

- **Tool keys.** The toolbar and the Tools menu each bound B, E, I, V, T, P, S, C, H, Z, Ctrl+B, Shift+R/E/I/P and Ctrl+T. G was the toolbar's Gradient and the menu's Fill. The menu's two unique keys, U and R, were the only tool keys that worked.
- **Folder-tab keys.** The main window's folder-tab `Ctrl+T` / `Ctrl+W` / `Ctrl+Tab` / `Ctrl+Shift+Tab` shared keys with Paint's Transform, Close Tab and document-tab cycling.
- **`Ctrl+Shift+N`.** New Comic Project and Add Layer both used it.
- **`Q`.** Quick Select (toolbar) collided with Quick Mask Mode.

Fix:

- The Tools menu is now the only owner of tool keys; `tool_shortcut()` looks them up. The toolbar shows the key in its tooltip without binding it, because a hidden toolbar's shortcuts stop working and the menu can't be hidden. `TOOL_ORDER` is a plain id list. Rect/Lasso/Wand (M/L/W) moved onto the menu. Keys follow the documented table: Fill G, Gradient U, Smudge R. Quick Select has no key, matching the docs.
- The folder-tab shortcuts are enabled only on the Imervue tab. Their tab bar lives there.
- New Comic Project uses `Ctrl+Alt+N`; `Ctrl+Shift+N` stays Add Layer, as documented.
- The paint key table in all 10 READMEs was wrong: R listed as rect select, S as smudge, C missing. It now matches the keys, and M/L/W were added.

Verification:

- A live re-scan of every main tab finds 0 conflicts, and each probed key fires its action.
- `tests/test_main_window_shortcut_conflicts.py` fails on the old code, listing the 22 Paint keys and the missing attribute.
- Full suite: 11,948 passed, 1 skipped. Ruff and bandit are clean.

## U-20260924-62 · 2026-09-24 · Paint shortcut remaps apply to every registry entry, live · #fix #paint #shortcuts

The Paint "Keyboard Shortcuts" dialog saved remaps for 23 registry entries, but only four were ever applied: fit, actual size, colour swap and colour reset. Even those were read once, when the workspace was built. Remapping a tool, Add / Duplicate / Merge Layer, Undo / Redo, the brush-size brackets or Deselect saved a value that did nothing. Two registry keys were advertised but bound nowhere: `Ctrl+[` / `Ctrl+]` (move layer), named in the Layers dock tooltips, and `Ctrl+D` (deselect).

- **New module `Imervue/paint/shortcut_binding.py`.**
  - `tag_registry_shortcut` marks the QAction or QShortcut that owns an entry.
  - `registry_shortcut` builds a QShortcut on the entry's default key.
  - `apply_registry_bindings` rebinds every tagged object under a widget. It replaces only the registry's key, so Redo keeps its `Ctrl+Y` alias.
- **Tagging.** The Tools menu, Layer menu and Edit menu tag their actions. The workspace builds its bracket, view, colour, layer-move and deselect keys through `registry_shortcut`, which removed the old `_registry_key` fallback path.
- **When remaps apply.** `PaintWorkspace.apply_shortcut_registry` runs when the workspace is built and again when the dialog is accepted. It also refreshes the toolbar tooltips (`PaintToolBar.show_tool_keys`) and the Layers dock button tooltips (`show_shortcuts`).
- **Registry helper.** `shortcut_registry.default_key` was added.
- **Newly bound keys.** `Ctrl+[` / `Ctrl+]` move the active layer and `Ctrl+D` deselects. They were added to the paint key table in all 10 READMEs.

Verification:

- A live window with a saved `paint.tool.brush = Y` switches to Brush on Y, and B no longer does. `Ctrl+]` and `Ctrl+D` fire.
- A re-scan of every main tab still finds 0 conflicts.
- `tests/test_paint_shortcut_binding.py` checks that every registry entry has exactly one owner on its default key. It also covers remaps reaching actions, QShortcuts and tooltips, saved remaps at build time, and dialog acceptance.
- Full suite: 11,961 passed, 1 skipped. Ruff and bandit are clean.

The Paint shortcut dialog only flagged a remap that collided with another registry row. Remapping, say, Brush to `Ctrl+S` or `M` produced no warning, yet it left both keys dead. Two enabled shortcuts on one key are ambiguous to Qt, which fires neither.

## U-20260924-65 · 2026-09-24 · Qt's own dialog and widget strings follow the UI language · #i18n #feature

Qt's built-in widgets take their text from Qt's own catalogue, and Imervue never installed a `QTranslator`. So in every language, Qt's own text stayed English:

- dialog-button-box OK / Cancel;
- message-box Yes / No;
- the file dialog;
- the Paint tab bar's close-button tooltip ("Close Tab");
- line-edit context menus;
- the key-sequence editor's "Press shortcut".

The earlier runtime scan of the Traditional Chinese UI surfaced "Close Tab" and "Press shortcut".

- New `Imervue/system/qt_translations.py`:
  - `qt_locale_for` maps the language codes to zh_TW / zh_CN / ja / ko.
  - `install_qt_translations(app, language)` loads `qtbase_<locale>` from PySide6's `TranslationsPath`, which the wheel ships, and installs it.
  - A second call replaces the first. English and plugin languages leave no translator installed.
  - A missing catalogue logs a warning and the app stays English.
- `ImervueMainWindow` installs it right after `reset_language`. A language change already asks for a restart.
- Packaging: Nuitka includes Qt translations by default (its opt-out is `--noinclude-qt-translations`, which release.yml does not pass), and PyInstaller's PySide6 hook collects them.

Tests (`tests/test_qt_translations.py`):

- the locale mapping;
- 確定 / 取消 and 關閉分頁 under Traditional Chinese;
- a catalogue exists for every built-in language;
- replacement, and removal under English;
- the missing-catalogue warning.

Full suite: 11,980 passed, 1 skipped. Ruff and bandit are clean.

## U-20260924-68 · 2026-09-24 · Offer every viewer format in Open and Relocate, and translate file-dialog filter labels · #i18n #fix

**Open Image and Relocate Missing File now list every format the viewer opens.** Their filters used to be typed by hand, in four copies:

- Open Image (`menu/file_menu.py`);
- Relocate Missing File in the right-click menu, the list view and the issue panel.

None of the copies had HEIC / HEIF / AVIF, JPEG XL or video, although the viewer opens all of them. A user could not pick an `.heic` or `.jxl` file in those dialogs without typing `*` first.

The filters now come from `image_loader.SUPPORTED_EXTENSIONS` (renamed from the private `_SUPPORTED_EXTS`), so a format the loader learns shows up in the dialogs with no other edit.

**File-dialog filter labels are translated.** Only one filter in the app had a translation, and that translation carried its own copy of the patterns. The rest showed "Images", "Puppet files", "Pet script", "ICC Profiles" and "Comic Book Zip" in English in every language.

The new `Imervue/gui/file_filters.py` builds each filter as a translated label plus patterns that stay in code:

- `name_filter`;
- `translated_filter`;
- `image_filter`;
- `viewer_filter`.

The changes:

- 16 modules route their filters through it: the tool dialogs, Paint, Puppet, Desktop Pet and annotation.
- `dialog_rows.IMAGE_SAVE_FILTER` became `image_save_filter()`. As a constant it was built once at import time and would have kept the start-up language.
- Seven `file_filter_*` keys were added to the five dictionaries.
- The dead `reference_panel_filter` key was removed.

Labels that are format or product names (PNG, PDF, Photoshop, Cube LUT, Cubism …) stay as they are.

`tests/test_file_filters.py` covers:

- the builders, including labels following the UI language while patterns stay put;
- that the viewer filter equals the loader's extension set;
- that the Open Image dialog offers HEIC and JXL;
- a scan that fails on any new hard-coded filter label with ordinary words.

**Runtime checks before this change.** In a throwaway worktree, with the dialogs auto-closed and device actions skipped (mic, webcam, virtual camera, NDI, VTS, global hotkeys):

- every Puppet menu action was triggered once, in English and in Traditional Chinese;
- every Desktop Pet control was driven, including the script editor's buttons, clicks and drags on the pet window, and the tray menu.

Both reported no exceptions and no error logs. The Traditional Chinese harvest of those dialogs found no untranslated text except these file filters.

## U-20260924-69 · 2026-09-24 · Count, not time, the pump_until immediate-return test · #test #flaky

`tests/test_conftest_pump_until.py::test_returns_true_as_soon_as_the_predicate_holds` failed once in a full parallel run: `pump_until(lambda: True)` took 1.08 s against a 1.0 s bound. That run took 55 minutes instead of the usual 8, so the machine was heavily loaded. On such a machine a single `processEvents()` pass on the shared application can take longer than a second. The file passes on its own in 0.7 s.

The test now counts instead of timing. `time.sleep` is patched to record calls, and the test asserts:

- the predicate was called exactly once;
- nothing slept.

That is what "returns as soon as the predicate holds" means, and machine load no longer affects the check. The other three tests in the file keep their timing checks: they only assert lower bounds or generous upper bounds on a deliberate wait.

## U-20260924-70 · 2026-09-24 · Show, drop and index HEIC, JPEG XL and video wherever the viewer opens them · #fix #formats

**The file tree, drag-and-drop and the library now accept every format the viewer opens.** Each had its own hand-typed extension list, and each missed something.

| Where | Was missing | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| File tree (`main_window_layout._build_file_tree`) | HEIC / HEIF / AVIF, JPEG XL, video | those files were hidden from the tree |
| Main-window drop (`_SUPPORTED_DROP_EXTS`) | same | dropping them did nothing |
| Library scanner (`scanner._IMAGE_EXTS`) | HEIC / AVIF, JPEG XL | never indexed |
| Library Maintenance (`maintenance._SCAN_EXTS`) | SVG | — |

The scanner and maintenance lists also disagreed with each other. Maintenance counted every HEIC and JXL file as "new", and no rescan ever indexed them, so the count never went down.

The new `Imervue/image/formats.py` is the one place these sets are defined:

- `RAW_EXTENSIONS`;
- `STILL_IMAGE_EXTENSIONS`, used by the library;
- `VIEWER_EXTENSIONS`, the still images plus video, used by the viewer, the file tree, drag-and-drop and the Open / Relocate dialogs;
- `ensure_pillow_opener(ext)`, moved out of `image_loader`.

Other changes:

- `image_loader.SUPPORTED_EXTENSIONS` (from the previous commit) and `_RAW_EXTS` are gone.
- The thumbnail worker and the Filter menu's RAW group use `RAW_EXTENSIONS`.
- The scanner's walk is now `maintenance.scan_image_files`, so what Maintenance reports as new is exactly what a rescan indexes.
- The scanner registers the HEIF / JXL codec before reading a file, so those files get their size and perceptual hash.

The folder-preview list in `folder_thumbnail_model.PREVIEW_EXTS` stays separate on purpose: it holds only formats `QImage` decodes. The CLI's list stays separate too, because it only handles what Pillow reads.

New and updated tests:

- `tests/test_formats.py`: how the sets nest, and which codec each extension registers.
- `test_main_window_drop`: HEIC, AVIF, JXL and MP4 are accepted.
- `test_main_window_layout`: the tree's name filters equal `VIEWER_EXTENSIONS`.
- `test_library_scanner`: the scanner walks exactly what Maintenance diffs against, and registers the codec before it reads.
- `test_maintenance`: every still format is scanned, and video is not.

## U-20260924-71 · 2026-09-24 · Read the capture date from the Exif sub-IFD in Organizer, Timeline and Sanitize · #fix #exif

**Three features now find a camera's capture date.** They each read the date with `Image.getexif().get(36867)`:

- Image Organizer's "by date" rule;
- the Timeline view;
- Image Sanitize's date-based file names.

Pillow's `getexif()` returns only IFD0. Cameras store `DateTimeOriginal` (36867) in the Exif sub-IFD (0x8769), so the lookup always missed. All three then fell back to IFD0's `DateTime` (306). That tag is the last-modified stamp, and editors rewrite it. When a file had neither tag, they fell back to its modification time. I confirmed the miss by saving a JPEG whose only date is the sub-IFD `DateTimeOriginal`: `getexif().get(36867)` returns `None`.

`library.calendar_index.capture_datetime()` already read the dates correctly (the sub-IFD first, then IFD0, then mtime). The three copies now call it, and each keeps its own fallback when no date is found:

- Organizer: the `unknown` folder;
- Timeline: the epoch;
- Sanitize: now.

`capture_datetime` also gained two things:

- It registers the HEIF / JXL codec first, so HEIC files have their EXIF read.
- It accepts the `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` form that Sanitize used to accept and some writers emit.

Tests added:

- For Organizer, Timeline and Sanitize, a JPEG whose only date is the sub-IFD `DateTimeOriginal` must be dated from it. Organizer and Timeline also have a missing-file fallback test.
- For `calendar_index`: a real HEIC file's capture time, the dashed format, and that the codec is registered before the read.

## U-20260924-72 · 2026-09-24 · Show EXIF and GPS for HEIC and JPEG XL photos · #fix #exif #formats

**HEIC and JPEG XL photos now show their EXIF.** `image.info.get_exif_data()` read `img._getexif()`, a private helper that only Pillow's JPEG, PNG and WebP plugins provide. pillow-heif's `HeifImageFile` has no such helper, so every HEIC photo came back with `{}`. That covers iPhone photos, and JPEG XL too. The same empty dict reached every consumer:

- the EXIF sidebar;
- the on-screen EXIF overlay;
- the Image Info dialog;
- smart albums' EXIF rules;
- the MCP read tool;
- Date Import, which fell back to the file mtime.

A throwaway check confirmed it. A HEIC file saved with Make and DateTimeOriginal showed both through `getexif()`, while `get_exif_data()` returned `{}`.

The fix has two parts:

- `get_exif_data()` now builds its tag dict from the public `getexif()`. It merges the Exif sub-IFD and nests the GPS sub-IFD under `GPSInfo`, which is how Pillow's own `_getexif()` builds its dict (`Exif._get_merged_dict`), so JPEG results do not change. A test compares the two tag for tag on a camera-like JPEG.
- It registers the HEIF / JXL codec first, through `formats.ensure_pillow_opener`. Before, a HEIC file opened before anything else had registered pillow-heif could not be read at all.

`image.gps.extract_gps()` already used `getexif()`, but it never registered the codec either. It now does, so the map view places HEIC photos.

Tests added:

- `tests/test_image_info.py`:
  - JPEG output equals Pillow's merged view;
  - a real HEIC file's Make, DateTimeOriginal and GPS are read;
  - the codec is registered before the read.
- `tests/test_gps.py`:
  - a real HEIC file's coordinates are read (southern hemisphere, so the sign is checked too);
  - the codec is registered before the read.

## U-20260924-73 · 2026-09-24 · Export the camera EXIF columns from the Exif sub-IFD · #fix #exif

**The metadata export now fills its camera columns.** The export writes eight EXIF fields to CSV / JSON: DateTimeOriginal, Make, Model, LensModel, FocalLength, FNumber, ExposureTime and ISOSpeedRatings. It read them with `Image.getexif()`, which returns IFD0 only. Only Make and Model live in IFD0; the other six are in the Exif sub-IFD, so those columns were always empty.

The changes:

- **New `Imervue/image/exif_merge.py`.** It holds `merged_exif(img)`: IFD0 merged with the Exif sub-IFD, with the GPS sub-IFD nested under `GPSInfo`. That is the same shape as Pillow's JPEG `_getexif()`, but it works for every format. It is the helper `image.info` gained in the previous commit, moved out so a module outside `info` can share it without importing Qt.
- **`metadata_export` uses it, and registers the HEIF / JXL codec first.** HEIC and JXL files get their fields too.
- **Rational values become numbers.** ExposureTime, FNumber and FocalLength now come out as `IFDRational`, and JSON's `default=str` would have written them as strings. They are now converted to `float`. A zero denominator, which EXIF uses for "unknown", becomes `None` rather than NaN (NaN is not valid JSON).

Tests added:

- `tests/test_metadata_export.py`:
  - a camera-like JPEG exports all eight fields with the expected values;
  - JSON holds real numbers;
  - `_coerce_value` covers every branch.
- `tests/test_exif_merge.py`:
  - the result equals Pillow's `_getexif()` on JPEG;
  - there is no GPS entry when the image has no GPS;
  - an image without EXIF gives an empty dict.

## U-20260924-74 · 2026-09-24 · Read RAW pixel dimensions through libraw instead of the embedded preview · #fix #raw

**RAW files now report their real pixel size everywhere Imervue reads dimensions from a file header.** Nine places read the size with `Image.open(path).size`. Pillow opens CR2, NEF, ARW and DNG as TIFF and reports the size of the embedded preview. I checked with rawpy's own CR2 test file (`M0054341_01_00005.cr2`): Pillow reported 1936 × 1288, and libraw reported the real 3906 × 2602. As a result, RAW photos got:

- the preview's size in the Image Info dialog and in the library index;
- the wrong resolution bucket in Image Organizer;
- wrong answers from the Advanced Filter and smart-album dimension / aspect rules;
- the wrong resolution in Duplicate Detection's pick of the largest copy;
- the wrong values in the metadata export and the rename `{width}` / `{height}` tokens.

The fix has two parts:

- `raw_loader.raw_dimensions(path)` reads libraw's header parse: `open_file` without `unpack`, which took 8 ms on that file. For a quarter-turn orientation (`flip` 5 / 6) it swaps the sides, as the developed image does.
- The new `Imervue/image/dimensions.py` provides `image_dimensions(path)`. RAW goes to libraw; everything else goes to Pillow after the HEIF / JXL codec is registered.

These now call it:

- the library scanner;
- Image Organizer;
- the metadata export;
- token rename;
- the Advanced Filter;
- Duplicate Detection;
- smart albums;
- the browser metadata index;
- Image Info (it keeps its thumbnail-shape fallback for a file nothing can read).

Tests:

- `tests/test_image_raw_loader.py`: a stand-in rawpy checks the header-only call order (`open_file`, `close`, no `unpack`), all four orientations, and that an unsupported file returns `None` and still closes. An unexpected error is let through.
- `tests/test_image_dimensions.py`: raster sizes, RAW dispatch even when Pillow could open the file, missing and undecodable files, a real HEIC, and codec registration.
- Two organizer tests patched `Image.open` through the module's own `Image` name, which the module no longer imports. They now patch `PIL.Image` directly: the same target with the same assertion.

## U-20260924-75 · 2026-09-24 · Let Image Organizer sort HEIC, JPEG XL and RAW photos · #feature #formats

**Image Organizer now sorts HEIC, AVIF, JPEG XL and camera RAW photos too.** Its folder scan used a hand-typed list of raster formats, so an iPhone or camera folder was organized with its HEIC and RAW files left where they were. It now scans `formats.STILL_IMAGE_EXTENSIONS`: every still format the viewer opens. It still takes no video.

The two earlier commits made this safe:

- The date rule reads the real capture time for these files (Exif sub-IFD, HEIF codec registered).
- The resolution rule reads their real size (libraw for RAW).

Files nothing can read still fall back as before: their modification time for the date, and `unknown` for the resolution. The type rule buckets them as `HEIC`, `CR2` and so on.

`tests/test_image_organizer.py` checks that the scan takes HEIC, CR2, NEF (in upper case), JXL, AVIF and PNG, and leaves out MP4 and text.

## U-20260924-76 · 2026-09-24 · Fix the viewer reload ImportError and check every internal import resolves · #fix #regression #test

**Regression fix for U-20260924-70.** That change removed `image_loader._RAW_EXTS` in favour of `formats.RAW_EXTENSIONS`. It missed one user, `DeepZoomLoadingMixin._should_progressive_decode`, which imports the name inside the function. From that commit on, every call to `load_deep_zoom_image` raised `ImportError`. That covers reloading the current image with its recipe, which is what the color-blindness preview modes, recipe edits and similar actions do. The full suite stayed green because no test reached that method.

It was found by triggering every main-window menu action in a throwaway worktree. The three color-blindness preview entries each raised the `ImportError`. After the fix, the same run triggers 172 actions with 0 errors.

The fix:

- `_should_progressive_decode` imports `RAW_EXTENSIONS` from `Imervue.image.formats`.
- `tests/test_deep_zoom_loading.py` covers the method: RAW files (any case), a large file, a small file and a missing file.

The same kind of break cannot pass silently again. `tests/test_internal_imports_resolve.py` parses every module under `Imervue/` and `plugins/`, collects every absolute `from Imervue... import name`, including the ones inside functions, and checks that each name exists in its module (as an attribute, or as a submodule of a package). It runs over 822 files, and they all resolve now. Its own tests check that:

- the detector finds function-level imports;
- the resolver accepts names and submodules;
- the resolver rejects the removed `_RAW_EXTS`.

`architecture_explore.md` §12 gains trap 13: function-level imports and how the new test guards them.

The full suite passed with 12,871 tests and 3 setup errors. All three errors are in `test_puppet_canvas_render.py`'s shared fixture, and none comes from this change. While that fixture waited on the event loop, a `QTimer.singleShot(50, lambda: self._connect_screen_change_signal(...))` fired. Another test file had armed it for a main window that was already deleted, and `RuntimeError: Internal C++ object already deleted` followed. The next commit fixes that timer class.

## U-20260924-77 · 2026-09-24 · Drop deferred calls whose widget was destroyed · #fix #qt

**Deferred calls no longer run after their widget is gone.** Fourteen places deferred work with `QTimer.singleShot(ms, lambda: self....)` or a local closure. A lambda belongs to no `QObject`, so Qt keeps the call even after the object it touches is destroyed. When it fires, it reaches a deleted C++ object: `RuntimeError: Internal C++ object (ImervueMainWindow) already deleted`. A throwaway check confirmed the behaviour:

- a bare lambda runs after its object was deleted;
- `singleShot(ms, context, fn)` and a bound method of a `QObject` are dropped.

This is how the previous commit's full run failed three `test_puppet_canvas_render` setups. A main window built by another test file had armed `_connect_screen_change_signal`'s 50 ms retry, and the retry fired after that window was deleted. In the app, the same happens when a window, dialog or viewer closes while a retry, settle watch or rearm timer is still pending.

The fix:

- **New `Imervue/system/qt_timers.py`.** It holds `call_later(ms, owner, fn)`, which uses the context overload so Qt cancels the call when `owner` is destroyed. A non-`QObject` owner (a test double) gets a plain timer, which is what those call sites had before.
- **Call sites routed through it, with owners:**
  - the viewer: deep-zoom preview start and retry, zoom-limit rearm, thumbnail retry, and both `view_fitting` settle chains;
  - the main window: startup folder open, list scroll restore, deep-zoom restore retry and the screen-signal retry;
  - the Modify splitter's re-size chain;
  - the puppet motion-timeline handles;
  - the batch exporter's stop timer;
  - the command-line file open.
- **Motion-timeline owner.** The drag handles' deferred model update belongs to the timeline view, not to the handle. The update itself rebuilds the scene and deletes that handle.
- **`settle_poll.poll_settle` takes `owner=`.** The viewer's settle watches pass the viewer; the Modify splitter's passes the splitter. The chain ends when that object is destroyed, before `still_current` can touch it.
- **`__main__`** keeps its import inside the function, so start-up import order does not change.

Tests added:

- `tests/test_qt_timers.py`:
  - the call runs while the owner is alive;
  - it is dropped once the owner is destroyed;
  - a non-`QObject` owner still runs;
  - an AST scan rejects any new `QTimer.singleShot(ms, lambda ...)` in `Imervue/` and `plugins/`.
- `tests/test_settle_poll.py`: a chain whose owner is destroyed runs no pass.
- `tests/test_main_window_screen_adapt.py`: the screen-signal retry stops once its window is deleted. The test uses a never-shown window, so `windowHandle()` stays `None` and the method would otherwise keep retrying.

`architecture_explore.md` §10.5 now carries the rule, and there is a row for the new module.

## U-20260924-78 · 2026-09-24 · Show EXIF-tagged photos upright across the viewer and Modify · #fix #exif

**Photos tagged with an EXIF orientation now display upright.** Phones and cameras usually store a portrait shot as landscape pixels plus an Orientation tag (for example `6`, "rotate 90° clockwise to view"). Imervue ignored the tag everywhere it showed an image, so every such photo lay on its side:

- the viewer's full-resolution load and its preview (`load_image_file`);
- the thumbnail worker;
- the list view's thumbnails;
- the hover preview;
- the Modify tab's source;
- the annotation dialog.

The only way to see them upright was the right-click "Auto-orient", which writes a separate `_oriented.png`. I checked with a JPEG of 40 × 20 stored pixels tagged `6`: `load_image_file` returned a 20 × 40 (h × w) landscape array.

The same gap corrupted files, not just the display. The annotation dialog and the Modify tab's "apply crop" save the pixels back without EXIF. Saving a portrait phone photo from either one dropped its tag and left the pixels sideways, so the photo was sideways in every program from then on. Both now start from the upright pixels, so the saved file is upright.

What changed:

- **`image.orientation`** gains `exif_orientation(img)`, `transpose_for(img, code)` and `upright(img)`. Its transpose table gives the same result as the existing `transform_for_orientation` for all eight codes, and a test checks this. Unlike `ImageOps.exif_transpose`, `transpose_for` returns the image itself when no turn is needed, so an already-upright full-resolution load is not copied.
- **Every path listed above now turns the image upright.** HEIC is already turned by pillow-heif on open, which sets the tag to 1. RAW is turned by libraw. Neither is turned twice.
- **Existing develop edits keep their meaning.** A recipe's rotate / flip / crop was drawn on whatever pixels Imervue showed, which for tagged photos were the sideways ones. `Recipe` gains `exif_oriented`, which is `True` for new recipes. A saved recipe without the key reads as `False`. `Recipe.base_is_oriented()` is `False` only for such a legacy recipe that carries geometry, and only then do the loader, the thumbnail worker and the Modify tab skip the turn. Legacy tone-only recipes do not depend on orientation, so those photos load upright too. The real recipe store on this machine holds 3 recipes, none with geometry.
- **The thumbnail disk cache key gains a version** (`_KEY_VERSION = 2`), so thumbnails cached sideways stop matching and are rebuilt.
- **`image_dimensions` reports the upright size**: a quarter-turn tag swaps the sides, as the RAW path already did for libraw's `flip`.
- **The hover preview's caption shows the image's own size.** It used to print the scaled preview's size, so a 900 × 600 image read as 512 × 341. The list view's resolution column uses `image_dimensions`, which also fixes the RAW sizes there.

README (all ten languages) now describes EXIF orientation under Viewer.

Tests:

- **`tests/test_orientation.py`**: the Pillow transpose matches the array transform for codes 1–8; unknown codes return the same object; reading the tag; `upright`.
- **`tests/test_image_loader.py`**:
  - tagged photos load upright, full size and as a thumbnail, with the red half on top;
  - a new recipe's crop applies to the upright image;
  - a legacy geometry recipe keeps the stored orientation;
  - a legacy tone-only recipe loads upright.
- **`tests/test_load_thumbnail_worker.py`**: the thumbnail is upright, and a legacy geometry recipe keeps the stored orientation.
- **`tests/test_recipe.py`**: the flag's defaults, legacy reading, round-trip through `to_dict` and `normalized`, and that the flag is not an edit.
- **`tests/test_develop_panel.py`**: the Modify source is upright, and the decode cache is keyed by orientation.
- **`tests/test_annotation_dialog.py`**: the dialog receives the upright image.
- **`tests/test_hover_preview.py`**: the preview is upright, and the caption shows 900 × 600 for a 512-wide preview.
- **`tests/test_image_dimensions.py`**: the upright size.
- **`tests/test_thumbnail_disk_cache.py`**: the key depends on its version.

## U-20260924-79 · 2026-09-24 · Check plugin-to-plugin imports resolve too · #test

`tests/test_internal_imports_resolve.py` (U-20260924-76) checked only `from Imervue... import name`. The bundled plugins also import each other's modules by their package name, for example `from ai_denoise.denoise import ...`, and `conftest` puts `plugins/` on `sys.path` for that. There are 238 such imports across 17 plugins, and a rename inside a plugin that missed one of them would have gone unnoticed in the same way.

The test now treats the app and every bundled plugin package (a directory with `__init__.py`) as a root, and resolves imports from all of them.

A module that cannot load because an **optional third-party** dependency is missing (onnxruntime, torch) is skipped, since there is nothing to check on this machine. A missing module under one of our own roots still fails. New cases cover:

- that the plugin roots are included;
- that a missing `Imervue` submodule fails.

All 825 files resolve.

## U-20260924-80 · 2026-09-24 · Load tool inputs and the rotate fallback from the EXIF-upright image · #fix #exif

**The single-image tools and the rotate fallback now start from the upright image.** This continues the previous commit, which made the viewer show EXIF-tagged photos upright.

- **`_apply_save.load_rgba(path)` now returns the EXIF-upright array.**
  - About 95 call sites load their input through it: the apply-and-save tool dialogs, and ten plugins in Imervue_Plugins.
  - Those tools save their result without EXIF. So for a portrait phone photo, both the tool's preview and the saved copy lay on their side, for good.
  - The signature is unchanged. The change is recorded in `architecture.md` §6, where the plugins' use of the function is listed.
  - The plugins do not touch EXIF, and nothing in the app saves with `exif=` or copies `img.info`, so no output is turned twice.
- **`lossless_rotate._rotate_via_pil` now rotates from what is shown.**
  - This is the fallback for non-JPEG files, and for JPEG when piexif is missing.
  - It used to rotate the stored pixels and re-save without EXIF. On a tagged image, dropping the tag undid the rotation, so the image appeared not to turn at all.

Left out on purpose:

- **MCP's `load_rgba_array`.** A client picks crop coordinates from the size the MCP read tools report. The loader and that report need to change together, so they stay as they are in this commit.
- **The dialogs that open files with Pillow directly** (crop / straighten, healing, clone stamp, lens correction …). They are a separate sweep.

Tests:

- **`tests/test_apply_save.py`**: `load_rgba` turns a tag-6 PNG so that the stored left half ends up on top.
- **`tests/test_lossless_rotate.py`**: the Pillow rotate on a tagged PNG turns what is shown by a real quarter turn.

## U-20260924-81 · 2026-09-24 · Load the hand-loading tool dialogs and image layers upright · #fix #exif

**The last tools that loaded the current image by hand now load it upright too.** Eight tool dialogs read it with `np.asarray(Image.open(src).convert("RGBA"))` in their worker, or `Image.open(path).convert("RGBA")` for their preview:

- auto straighten (detect and apply);
- clone stamp;
- crop / straighten;
- healing brush;
- lens correction;
- noise / sharpen;
- sky replace;
- soft proof.

That load skipped the EXIF turn the previous commits added everywhere else, so on a tagged portrait photo each of these tools showed a sideways preview and saved a sideways copy. The clone-stamp and healing preview points were placed on those sideways pixels too. The inline form also never closed the file, and on Windows the source stays locked until garbage collection.

The changes:

- All eight now call `_apply_save.load_rgba`, which turns the image upright and closes the file. The preview and the worker therefore agree on the orientation.
- Crop / straighten's aspect presets read the upright size through `image_dimensions` instead of the stored `Image.open(...).size`, so a 16:9 preset on a portrait photo is computed on the portrait sides.
- `image/layers.py` image layers (an overlay file placed on a develop recipe) are turned upright as well. Being pure logic, that module uses `image.orientation` directly rather than the `gui` helper.

Tests:

- **`tests/test_crop_straighten_dialog.py`**: the aspect is taken from the upright size, and the worker saves the upright image.
- **`tests/test_layers.py`**: an image layer is placed upright.
- **`tests/test_apply_save.py`**: a scan fails if any module in `Imervue/gui/` goes back to an inline `Image.open(p).convert("RGBA")`.

## U-20260924-82 · 2026-09-24 · Keep converted, exported, stripped and sanitised copies upright · #fix #exif

**Converted, exported, stripped and sanitised copies of a tagged photo are now upright.** These tools write their output without EXIF:

- Batch Convert;
- Export;
- Batch Export;
- EXIF Strip;
- Image Sanitize.

For a portrait phone photo, the stored pixels are sideways and only the Orientation tag makes them upright. So every output lay on its side, and nothing could turn it back. I checked with a tag-6 JPEG: `save_image` to JPEG, PNG, WebP and TIFF gave 40 × 20 with no tag in all four.

EXIF Strip was the worst case: it overwrites the original by default. Stripping a portrait photo turned the photo itself sideways for good.

What changed:

- **`image.orientation.transpose_for` now removes the orientation from the turned image.** It clears both the EXIF tag and the XMP `tiff:Orientation`, as Pillow's `exif_transpose` does. `transpose` copies `info`, so without this a later reader of the same image would turn it a second time.
- **New `Imervue/gui/export_source.py` with `open_export_source(path)`.** It replaces two near-duplicates: `export_dialog._open_image_for_export`, and `batch_export_dialog._open_for_export` plus `_apply_recipe`. It rasterises SVG, turns the image upright and applies the Develop recipe. As in the viewer, a legacy recipe with geometry stays on the stored orientation (`Recipe.base_is_oriented`).
- **Batch Convert, EXIF Strip and Image Sanitize** turn the image upright before they re-save or rebuild the pixels.

Other save paths (the right-click batch operations, the CLI, a few effect dialogs, MCP) come in a following commit.

Tests:

- **`tests/test_export_source.py`**:
  - a tagged photo comes out upright and without the tag;
  - a new recipe applies upright;
  - a legacy geometry recipe applies to the stored orientation;
  - untagged images come out as they were.
- **`tests/test_batch_convert.py`**, **`tests/test_exif_strip.py`**, **`tests/test_image_sanitize.py`**: a tagged portrait comes out 20 × 40. Strip also checks that the tag is gone.
- **`tests/test_orientation.py`**: the turned image reads as upright, and its XMP orientation is gone.
- **`tests/test_export_dialog.py`**: now patches the shared `open_export_source`.

## U-20260924-83 · 2026-09-24 · Keep batch-rotated, upscaled and retouched copies upright · #fix #exif

**The remaining save-a-copy paths now start from the upright image.** This continues the previous commits for tagged portrait photos:

- **Batch rotate (right-click).** It rotated the stored pixels and re-saved without EXIF. On a tagged image, dropping the tag undid the rotation, so the photo looked unturned. It now rotates what is shown, as `lossless_rotate`'s Pillow fallback already does.
- **AI Upscale.** Both the traditional resize and the Real-ESRGAN path now turn the image upright before scaling. The output carries no EXIF.
- **Auto colour balance, frequency separation and portrait retouch.** Each kept a private copy of `load_rgba`: `_load_rgba`, and `_load_image_as_rgba` in frequency separation. None of them turned the image or closed the file. The copies are deleted, and the three dialogs call the shared `_apply_save.load_rgba`.

Deliberately unchanged:

- **The headless CLI.** It has an explicit `auto-orient` command, the same convention as ImageMagick's `-auto-orient`, so its other commands keep their current, scriptable behaviour.
- **GIF / video frame sequences and deflicker.** They work on frame sequences, which do not carry a camera orientation.
- **MCP.** The loader and the size the read tools report need to change together.

Tests:

- **`tests/test_batch_ops.py`**: batch rotate on a tagged portrait gives a real quarter turn of what is shown.
- **`tests/test_ai_upscale.py`**: a tagged 10 × 8 photo upscaled ×2 comes out 16 × 20.

## U-20260924-84 · 2026-09-24 · Tie every deferred bound-method call to its owner · #fix #qt

**A deferred bound-method call no longer runs on a destroyed widget.** A full run failed at the setup of `tests/test_puppet_canvas_render.py` with `RuntimeError: Internal C++ object (ImervueMainWindow) already deleted`. The traceback was in `_connect_screen_change_signal` with `_retries=20`: the very first attempt, scheduled by `ImervueMainWindow` as `QTimer.singleShot(0, self._connect_screen_change_signal)`. A window built by an earlier test in the same worker had been deleted, and the queued call ran while the next fixture pumped the event loop.

`system/qt_timers` (added in b347f71) said a bound method of a `QObject` is cancelled automatically, and its guard only rejected lambdas. That is not what PySide6 6.11 does. I measured it offscreen: after the widget was deleted, `singleShot(0, w.method)` still ran a Python method (defined on a mixin or on the class itself). It also still ran the C++ slot `w.update`, which raised the same `RuntimeError`. Only the `singleShot(ms, context, callable)` overload is dropped with its context.

What changed:

- **Ten app call sites now go through `call_later(ms, owner, fn)`:**
  - the main window's screen-signal hook-up and its What's New popup;
  - the status bar's progress hide;
  - the canvas refresh after a screen change;
  - Batch Export's and GIF/Video's deferred `accept`;
  - Paint's deferred fit after show, and its View-menu signal hook-up (owner: the workspace);
  - the desktop pet's two canvas refreshes;
  - `SignalCoalescer.schedule`, which with its owner as context now also fires on the coalescer's own thread.
- **The `qt_timers` docstring and `architecture_explore.md` §10 are corrected** to say that bound methods need the owner too.
- **`tests/test_qt_timers.py`:**
  - records the measured behaviour: a bare bound-method `singleShot` still runs after its object is destroyed;
  - checks that `call_later` drops a bound method of a destroyed owner;
  - rejects any two-argument `singleShot(ms, obj.method)` under `Imervue/`.

The five plugin sites (`accept` after 0–500 ms) stay as they are. Plugins must run on releases that predate `call_later`, and that is noted in the test.

## U-20260924-85 · 2026-09-24 · Translate twelve hard-coded toasts and four f-string file filters · #i18n

**Twelve toasts and four file filters no longer show English in every language.** An AST scan found toasts that pass a literal or an f-string, either directly or through a local variable. The messages:

- batch rotate's "Rotated n/m file(s)";
- "Zoom limit: …";
- animation "Speed: …x";
- annotation "Load failed: …";
- "EXIF save failed: …";
- GIF/Video "Error: …" (its status label had the same text);
- file association "Error: …" (two places);
- "Session load failed: …";
- hierarchical tags "Tagged / Untagged n image(s) …".

They now go through new dictionary keys, and their English wording is unchanged: `batch_rotate_done`, `zoom_limit_toast`, `anim_speed_toast`, `annotation_load_failed`, `exif_save_failed`, `generic_error`, `tag_added_to_images`, `tag_removed_from_images`, `session_load_failed`.

The same pass turned up f-string file filters that the earlier filter guard missed, because it only read plain string constants:

- "Imervue session";
- Paint's "Imervue brush";
- the "raster paint apps brush" filter, now labelled "Brush from other paint apps";
- the "external image editors palette" filter, now "Palette from other image editors".

They go through `translated_filter` with new `file_filter_*` keys. The Chinese and Japanese wording reuses the Paint menu's existing terms: zh-TW 色票, zh-CN 色板. "Adobe Swatch" and "Adobe Swatch Exchange" are format names and stay as they are.

The keys were added to all five dictionaries.

Tests:

- **`tests/test_translation_keys_defined.py`** gains a toast detector, with its own detector test. A test fails on any toast that shows literal English, whether directly or through a local.
- **`tests/test_file_filters.py`**: the filter guard now reads f-strings, with placeholders standing in as `{}`, and matches both `(*.` and `(*{}`.
- **`tests/test_batch_ops.py`**: the rotate toast reads 已旋轉 1/1 個檔案 under Traditional Chinese.

## U-20260924-86 · 2026-09-24 · Run the MCP image tools on the EXIF-upright image · #fix #exif #mcp

**MCP tools now work on the EXIF-upright image, and report sizes in the same frame.** The viewer and the tool dialogs already show and save tagged photos upright (earlier commits today), but the MCP server still read the stored pixels.

For a tag-6 portrait photo that had three effects:

- `read_image_metadata` reported the landscape size;
- a client that picked a crop box from that size cropped the sideways pixels;
- every tool's output was saved sideways without EXIF. This covered crop, resize, rotate / flip, watermark, convert, the thumbnail, the effects and statistics that read through `load_rgba_array`, and sharpness.

What changed:

- **`mcp_server/tool_support.open_upright(path)`** returns the decoded image turned upright. Its turned copy carries no orientation tag, so nothing turns it again. `load_rgba_array` now goes through it.
- **Eight tool handlers switch to `open_upright`** from `Image.open`: four in `tools_read` and four in `tools_edit`.
- **`read_image_metadata` reports the upright width and height.** It swaps the sides for a quarter-turn tag, so the size a client reads is the frame that crop / resize use.
- **`QUARTER_TURN_CODES`** moves into `image.orientation` as a public constant. `image.dimensions` and MCP both use it instead of a private copy.
- **README (10 languages) and the Sphinx docs (10 languages)** now say, next to `crop_image`, that sizes and coordinates refer to the EXIF-upright image. All ten rst files parse without errors under docutils.

Tests in `tests/test_mcp_image_tools.py`:

- the metadata size of a tagged portrait is 20 × 40;
- a crop box in upright coordinates takes the red top half;
- `load_rgba_array` is upright.

## U-20260924-87 · 2026-09-24 · Translate the plugins' hard-coded error toasts · #i18n #Imervue_Plugins

**Three plugins no longer show a hard-coded English "Error: …".** They showed it in their status label and in the toast when a run failed:

- `ai_background_remover`;
- `object_splitter`;
- `safety_review` (the scan dialog).

They now use the main app's `generic_error` key: `self._lang.get("generic_error", "Error: {error}")`. On a release that predates the key, the fallback is the same English text as before, so a newly downloaded plugin still works on older installs.

`tests/test_translation_keys_defined.py` runs the toast detector over `plugins/` too, skipping the language packs. `test_plugin_translations_defined` already allows plugins to use keys from the main dictionary.

The three edited files are mirrored to Imervue_Plugins `main`, and the parity check reports no differences.

Tagged `#Imervue_Plugins` because the change ships through the plugin distribution repo.

## U-20260924-88 · 2026-09-24 · Compute smart crops on the recipe's base and show the last previews upright · #fix #exif

**The last previews that decoded a file themselves now show tagged photos upright, and smart crop no longer lands in the wrong place.**

**Smart crop was a regression from the EXIF-upright work.** It computed its suggestions on the stored, sideways pixels and wrote the chosen box into a new recipe. New recipes now apply to the upright image, so on a tagged portrait photo the crop cut the wrong region.

The fix is a new `export_source.recipe_base_image(path, recipe)`, which returns the pixels a recipe applies to:

- for a new recipe, or a missing one, the upright image;
- for a legacy recipe with geometry, the stored pixels.

Three places now use it:

- `open_export_source` is built on it;
- smart crop computes its suggestions on it;
- face detection finds its boxes on it, because those boxes are stored in the recipe too.

**Preview loaders now turn the image upright.** Six places opened the file with Pillow and ignored the tag:

- the compare view (`compare_dialog._load_rgba_array`);
- the image inspector;
- the reference panel preview;
- Duplicate Detection's list thumbnails;
- the timeline thumbnails;
- the drag-out image.

Each now reads the orientation from the open file, and turns the result after it is scaled down.

Left as they are:

- sort by resolution, because area does not change with orientation;
- the multi-page frame count;
- the library's analysis readers (phash, auto-tag, CLIP, culling);
- the multi-image merges (focus stack, HDR, panorama, stack blend).

Face boxes saved before this commit on a tagged photo were computed on the sideways pixels. Unless the recipe also carries geometry, the dialog now draws them on the upright image, so re-detect those.

Tests:

- **`tests/test_upright_previews.py`**: the six preview loaders and smart crop's loader each return a portrait result for a tag-6 photo, and the timeline worker emits a portrait thumbnail.
- **`tests/test_export_source.py`**: `recipe_base_image` is upright for a new or missing recipe, and stored for a legacy geometry recipe.
